Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a consistent position: computers are important in daily life and people have become dependent on them. The essay is easy to follow because each paragraph supports this main idea. You also use a strong opening question to attract the reader’s attention. This is a good foundation for an argumentative essay.

What you do well
Clear central argument  
   Your main claim is clear: computers have changed people’s lives and are now essential.

Logical organization  
   The essay moves in a sensible order:
   - computers are part of daily life,
   - people rely on them,
   - computers have changed society,
   - conclusion.

Relevant examples  
   You mention homes, schools, offices, and businesses, which helps show that computers are used everywhere.

Main areas to improve

Make the essay more argumentative
Right now, the essay mostly explains that computers are useful. An argumentative essay should do more than describe; it should make a claim and support it with stronger reasons, and possibly consider an opposing view.

For example, your thesis could be more specific:
Current idea: Computers have changed people’s lives.
Stronger thesis: Computers have become essential to modern life because they increase efficiency, improve access to information, and create new opportunities in education and work.

This gives your body paragraphs clearer purposes.

Improve paragraph development
Some paragraphs repeat similar ideas, especially “computers are useful” and “people depend on them.” Try to give each paragraph one main focus.

Possible structure:
Paragraph 1: Computers in daily life
Paragraph 2: Efficiency and productivity
Paragraph 3: Education and employment
Paragraph 4: Dependence and possible drawbacks

This will reduce repetition and make your argument stronger.

Use more precise vocabulary and grammar
There are several language errors that affect clarity. Below are some important corrections:

“For many people computers have become a part of their everyday life.”  
  Better: “For many people, computers have become part of everyday life.”

“In people's homes calculators as usually found”  
  Better: “In people’s homes, calculators are usually found”  
  or more naturally: “Many homes have calculators and even personal computers.”

“speed-up”  
  As a verb, write: “speed up” (no hyphen)

“laboreous”  
  Correct spelling: “laborious”

“Schools use computers to teach children because it is fun and exciting.”  
  Since the subject is plural, write:  
  “Schools use computers to teach children because they are fun and exciting.”  
  Even better: “Schools use computers because they make learning more interactive and engaging.”

“People's lives have changed significantly since the invention of the calculator (the first computer).”  
  This is factually inaccurate. A calculator is not the first computer. You could say:  
  “People’s lives have changed significantly since the invention of the computer.”

“help everyday life run smoothly”  
  Better: “help everyday life run more smoothly”

“When computers break down people are thrown back”  
  Add a comma after the introductory clause:  
  “When computers break down, people are thrown back…”

Be careful with repetition
The conclusion repeats the opening question and several earlier ideas. A conclusion should summarize the argument and leave the reader with a final thought.

For example:
“In conclusion, computers have become essential in homes, schools, and workplaces because they save time, increase productivity, and provide access to information. Although dependence on computers can create problems when systems fail, their importance in modern life is undeniable.”

This sounds more formal and argumentative.

Use a more academic tone
Some expressions are a little informal or too general, such as:
“wonderful things”
“fun and exciting”

Try more academic wording:
“valuable functions”
“increase engagement”
“improve efficiency”
“enhance productivity”

Suggested revision strategies
Rewrite your thesis to include 2–3 clear reasons.
Give each body paragraph one distinct main idea.
Remove repeated sentences about computers being useful.
Correct grammar and word choice, especially articles, verb forms, and punctuation.
Add one sentence about the drawbacks of dependence on computers to make the argument more balanced.

Example of a stronger thesis and topic sentences
Thesis: “Computers have become essential to modern life because they save time, improve education, and increase workplace productivity.”
Topic sentence 1: “First, computers are now common in everyday settings such as homes, schools, and shops.”
Topic sentence 2: “Second, computers allow people and businesses to complete tasks more efficiently.”
Topic sentence 3: “Finally, computer skills have become increasingly important for education and employment.”

Final comment
You have a solid basic essay with a clear message and understandable organization. Your next step is to make the writing more precise, less repetitive, and more clearly argumentative. With stronger paragraph focus and careful grammar revision, this essay can become much more effective.

Main problem: unclear thesis

At the moment, the essay seems to make several different claims:

Relativity changed human philosophy.
Humanity has not fully understood relativity.
Einstein’s ideas were used for atomic bombs.
Fear has shaped the modern global system.

These are all interesting, but the essay needs one clear central argument. Ask yourself: what exactly are you trying to prove?

A possible thesis could be:

Einstein’s theory of relativity transformed modern thought, but humanity has failed to apply its philosophical lessons positively, using scientific advances instead to build systems of fear and war.

If you choose something like this, then each paragraph should support that one claim.

Organization

Your essay needs a clearer structure. Right now, some ideas appear suddenly, and the connection between relativity and atomic bombs is not fully explained.

A stronger structure could be:

Introduction
Introduce Einstein and relativity.
State your thesis clearly.

Paragraph 1: Relativity and human understanding
Explain how relativity challenges fixed ideas of time, space, and certainty.
Show why this matters philosophically.

Paragraph 2: Personal/social understanding of time
Develop your idea that humans construct time socially.
Explain how relativity changes that understanding.

Paragraph 3: Misuse of scientific knowledge
Transition carefully: Einstein’s work contributed indirectly to nuclear technology.
Argue that humanity used scientific progress for war rather than peace.

Conclusion
Restate your argument.
End with your call for a shift from fear to peace.

Development of ideas

Some claims are too general or unclear. For example:

“The Socratic idea that all was known” is confusing and historically inaccurate. Socrates is more associated with questioning than with claiming complete knowledge.
“With this theory also came the area of quantum mechanics” is not correct. Relativity and quantum mechanics are different fields, though both are major developments in modern physics.
The connection between relativity and atomic bombs needs more precision. Einstein did not “invent” the bomb, though his work influenced modern physics and he signed a letter warning about nuclear weapons.

To strengthen your argument, be careful with facts and explain links more fully.

Language and style

You have many good ideas, but grammar, spelling, punctuation, and word choice often make the meaning hard to follow.

Common issues:
Spelling: “Einstien” → Einstein; “histories” → history’s; “nostolgic” → nostalgic; “Hawkings” → Hawking
Capitalization: “socratic” → Socratic
Sentence fragments: “The Socratic idea that all was known.” is incomplete.
Punctuation: avoid unnecessary semicolons and capital letters after semicolons.
Word choice: “set-up” should be set up as a verb; “indwell the topic” is unusual and unclear.

Example revisions

Original:
> Humanity witnessed one of histories most incredible minds at work when Albert Einstien came onto the scene.

Better:
> Humanity witnessed one of history’s most brilliant minds when Albert Einstein emerged on the world stage.

Original:
> The point is my notion of time has changed; And I think once humanity realizes that time-notions are human constructions and are subject to change, that then the impact of Einstien's idea will be felt.

Better:
> My own notion of time has changed, and I believe that when humanity recognizes that concepts of time are human constructions, the deeper impact of Einstein’s ideas will finally be felt.

Original:
> For now, we must deal with the short comings of humanity's use of Einstien's ideas: atomic weapons.

Better:
> For now, however, humanity must confront one of the darkest consequences of modern scientific knowledge: atomic weapons.

Tone and academic style

Try to use a more formal academic tone. Phrases like “came onto the scene” and “if you will” sound conversational. In academic writing, aim for precise and direct wording.

Also, if you mention examples such as Socrates, Stephen Hawking, or the Cold War, explain them briefly and accurately.

Revision priorities

Write one clear thesis.
Reorganize paragraphs so each one supports the thesis.
Correct factual inaccuracies about relativity, quantum mechanics, and atomic bombs.
Edit grammar, spelling, and punctuation carefully.
Use more precise academic language.

Final comment

This essay shows strong intellectual curiosity and a real desire to think deeply. That is an excellent quality in academic writing. Your next step is to make your ideas clearer, more accurate, and more organized so the reader can fully appreciate your argument.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: shopping malls have harmed Kalamazoo more than they have helped it. This is a strong argumentative topic, and you show good awareness of the relationship between transportation, class, suburban growth, and the decline of downtown areas. Your essay also uses a specific place, Kalamazoo, which makes the argument more vivid and concrete.

Strengths

Clear opinion
   Your thesis is easy to find: you believe shopping malls are “more of a detriment than a blessing.”

Good historical perspective
   You do more than simply say malls are bad. You explain how cities changed over time because of water routes, trains, cars, and suburban expansion.

Strong social concern
   One of the best parts of the essay is your attention to how poor and working-class people are affected. This gives your argument seriousness and purpose.

Main Areas to Revise

Make the thesis more specific
Your current thesis is understandable, but it is still broad. Try to say why malls are harmful.

For example:
“Although shopping malls are convenient, they have damaged downtown Kalamazoo by drawing businesses and wealthy residents away from the city center, which has hurt local neighborhoods and low-income residents.”

This version tells the reader your exact reasons.

Improve organization
Your ideas are interesting, but sometimes they move too quickly from one point to another. Try to organize the essay into clearer parts:

Introduction: introduce Kalamazoo and your thesis
Body paragraph 1: explain how downtown used to function
Body paragraph 2: explain how cars, suburbs, and malls pulled people and businesses away
Body paragraph 3: explain the social effects on poor residents
Conclusion: restate your argument and explain why it matters

Also, some sentences feel disconnected, such as:
“Everyone was near the central city - so everyone could use the central city.”
“and still could be!”

These ideas are good, but they should be integrated into complete paragraphs with formal transitions.

Use a more formal academic tone
Some phrases sound conversational or emotional rather than academic. For example:
“Why should we, say those with cars, go downtown…”
“nice indoor mall”
“and still could be!”

Instead of asking rhetorical questions, state the point directly:
“For suburban residents with cars, malls offered a more convenient and comfortable alternative to downtown shopping.”

This sounds stronger and more formal.

Focus on grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that make the essay harder to read. Below are some important ones:

“the could keep a distance” → “they could keep their distance”
“clientel” → “clientele”
“more poorer people” → “poorer people” or “the poor”
“Many larger factories and office building” → “Many large factories and office buildings”
“many business try” → “many businesses try”
“There neighborhoods” → “Their neighborhoods”
“flu from the inner cities” → probably “flight from the inner cities”
“upperclass” → “upper-class”

Also watch article use, plural forms, and sentence boundaries.

Correct awkward or unclear sentences
Some sentences contain good ideas but need rewriting for clarity. For example:

Original:
“This process has left the businesses in the inner cities, including Kalamazoo, without a stable clientel.”

Revised:
“This process has left businesses in inner cities, including Kalamazoo, without a stable clientele.”

Original:
“I believe it is only the storeowners who realize this process is occurring or enjoy the downtown district remain and stay in business.”

Revised:
“I believe that only storeowners who recognize this trend or who are personally committed to the downtown district continue to remain in business.”

Develop your evidence
Your argument would be stronger if you included more specific examples. For instance:
names of malls or shopping areas in Kalamazoo
examples of businesses that left downtown
examples of downtown festivals or college-related businesses
any facts, statistics, or observations about transportation and neighborhood decline

Even a few concrete examples would make your essay much more persuasive.

Sentence-Level Advice

Pay attention to:
subject-verb agreement: “businesses try,” not “business try”
word form: “wealthy,” “poorer,” “clientele”
capitalization and punctuation
avoiding sentence fragments like “and still could be!”

Try reading your essay aloud. This is a useful way to hear where sentences are too long, unclear, or incomplete.

Final Suggestion

Your essay has a thoughtful argument and a strong social message. The biggest improvements will come from:
making your thesis more precise,
organizing paragraphs more clearly,
using a more formal academic style,
correcting grammar and vocabulary errors.

You already have meaningful ideas. Now your task is to present them in a clearer, more polished way so the reader can fully appreciate your argument.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear argumentative position: television has great potential, but in practice it has mostly harmed society by encouraging passivity and weakening family life. This is a strong central idea, and your writing shows ambition in vocabulary and sentence structure. To revise successfully, focus on clarity, support, and accuracy.

Strengths

Clear thesis and argument
You take a definite position instead of only describing television.
The contrast between television’s potential benefits and its actual social effects is effective.

Logical overall structure
Paragraph 1 introduces the topic and the main claim.
Paragraphs 2–3 develop the argument.
Paragraph 4 concludes by restating the main idea.

Appropriate academic tone
Expressions like “On the contrary,” “therefore,” and “however” help create an argumentative style.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your main claim is interesting, but it is still a little broad. For example, “the one that has made the most impact” is a very large claim and may be difficult to prove. Narrow it.

Possible revision:
> Although television has increased access to information and culture, it has influenced society negatively by promoting passive entertainment, reducing family interaction, and discouraging active use of knowledge.

This version tells the reader exactly what your essay will argue.

B. Develop each body paragraph with clearer support
At the moment, some claims are strong but not fully explained. For example:
“T.V. is a major cause of lack of togetherness”
“has also resulted in an increase in laziness”

These are arguable points, but you need more explanation or examples. Ask yourself:
Why does television reduce togetherness?
How does it create laziness?
Can you give a specific example of family members sitting together physically but not communicating?

Try using this pattern in body paragraphs:
Topic sentence  
Explanation  
Example  
Link back to thesis

C. Avoid overgeneralizations
Some statements sound too absolute:
“most Americans”
“the only time that everyone is together”
“television has become the escape route of the masses”

These phrases make your essay sound dramatic, but they weaken academic credibility if you do not provide evidence. Use more cautious language:
“many Americans”
“in some families”
“for many viewers”

Academic writing is often stronger when it avoids claims that are impossible to prove.

Organization and coherence

Your ideas are generally connected, but transitions inside paragraphs could be smoother. For example, in paragraph 2, you move from educational benefits to social escape quite quickly. You can guide the reader more clearly.

Example transition:
> Despite these possibilities, television has not always fulfilled this educational role. Instead, it often functions as a form of escape from social and personal problems.

This makes the shift easier to follow.

Language corrections

You have good vocabulary, but there are several grammar, spelling, and word-choice errors. Below are important ones:

“has potential” → has the potential
“than cannot be given by newspaper” → that cannot be given by newspapers / by the newspaper
“staring at the T.V.” → staring at the TV
“lack of togetherness found in many American families” → better: the lack of closeness in many American families
“00 years ago” → 100 years ago
“integrety” → integrity
“dificulties” → difficulties
“has becomed” → has become

Grammar/style points
Article use
“by newspaper” → usually by newspapers or by the newspaper
“the television” is not necessary when speaking generally; usually just television

Word choice
“world views” usually means opinions or ideologies. You may mean people’s views of the world.
“creative vein” sounds unusual here. Better options:
  - creative medium
  - new form of artistic expression

Gender-neutral pronouns
“the less motivated he becomes” is grammatically old-fashioned. Better:
  - the less motivated a person becomes
  - the less motivated they become

Sentence-level suggestions

Here are two examples of possible revision:

Original:
> Television has the power to bring war into the home as well as artistic expressions of those involved in the program production, giving society the opportunity to understand their own culture more fully.

Revised:
> Television can bring major world events, such as war, into the home, and it can also present artistic expression through drama and documentary, helping people understand their own culture more fully.

Original:
> The entertainment aspect of television has offered society an easy escape from its problems and dificulties.

Revised:
> The entertainment aspect of television has given people an easy way to avoid confronting social and personal problems.

Final revision advice

For your next draft, focus on these three priorities:

Write a more specific thesis
Add explanation and examples for each major claim
Correct grammar, spelling, and word choice errors

You already have a strong foundation and a clear opinion. With more precise support and language correction, this essay can become much more persuasive and academically effective.

Feedback Report

You have a clear position: the polio vaccine was one of the most important inventions of the twentieth century because it reduced suffering and helped later medical progress. Your essay has a strong sense of importance and urgency, and your opening shows that you care about the topic. That passion is a strength in argumentative writing.

What is working well
Clear central claim  
   Your thesis is understandable: the polio vaccine changed society and encouraged further medical advances.

Good historical focus  
   You connect the vaccine to a specific time period and to social effects, which makes the argument more interesting than simply saying it “saved lives.”

Effective sense of audience  
   You compare the fears of different generations. This helps readers understand how serious polio once was.

Main areas to revise

Make the thesis more precise
Your current thesis is somewhat broad and emotional: “The effects of such a vaccine are countless and inequivalent...” Try to state exactly how it was important.

A stronger thesis could be:
> The polio vaccine was one of the most important inventions of the twentieth century because it dramatically reduced death and disability and helped build public confidence in modern vaccination.

This gives you two clear body points to develop.

Improve organization
Right now, the essay has an introduction and two body paragraphs, but the ideas sometimes move too quickly. Each paragraph should focus on one main idea.

Suggested structure:
Introduction: background on disease + thesis
Body paragraph 1: how polio harmed children and families; how the vaccine reduced death/disability
Body paragraph 2: how the vaccine influenced public health and later medical progress
Conclusion: restate significance

Use stronger evidence and avoid unsupported claims
Some statements are too general or questionable, for example:
“Every few years a new one comes along to normalize an overpopulated world.”
“The polio vaccine stopped death in its path.”
The connection between the vaccine and the Baby Boom is not clearly proven.

In an academic argument, avoid claims that sound absolute or speculative unless you provide evidence. Instead, use careful language:
“Infectious diseases have often influenced population growth.”
“The vaccine sharply reduced deaths and long-term disability from polio.”
“Although the Baby Boom had many causes, improved child health may have contributed to a growing surviving population.”

Use a more formal academic tone
Some phrases are too conversational:
“For sure”
“I’m sure”
“for each individual to decide”
“Things weren’t impossible any more”

Try replacing them with more formal expressions:
“Clearly”
“It is likely that”
“This remains debatable”
“The success of vaccination expanded the possibilities of medical science”

Also, avoid all caps like CAUSE unless you are quoting. In academic writing, emphasis should come from wording, not typography.

Correct grammar, spelling, and word choice
There are several language issues that interfere with clarity. Here are important examples:

“For centuries before” → “For centuries”
“mamed” → “maimed”
“medical & technological advances” → “medical and technological advances”
“starting at the middle of the century” → “beginning in the middle of the century”
“future’s children” → “future generations of children”
“20 year olds” → “twenty-year-olds”
“as a child” → “as children”
“The vaccine was utilized” → “The vaccine was first used”
“less children” → “fewer children”
“born during than time” → “born during that time”
“role of population control” → “role in population control”

Also watch punctuation. For example, you often have extra spaces, repeated words (“never never”), and sentence fragments.

Clarify scientific wording
You write:
> “injecting the dead virus to protect you from the virus itself...”

This idea is interesting, but be careful with accuracy. A clearer version:
> The success of vaccination showed that exposing the body to a safe form of the virus could build protection against the disease.

This sounds more precise and academic.

Example of a possible revision of your opening
> For centuries, disease caused the deaths and permanent disability of millions of people. Although disease still affects societies today, major medical advances in the twentieth century transformed human life. One of the most important of these advances was Jonas Salk’s polio vaccine. It was especially significant because it reduced the deaths and lifelong physical damage caused by polio and strengthened confidence in the power of modern medicine.

Final advice
When you revise, focus on these priorities:
Write a more specific thesis.
Organize each paragraph around one clear point.
Replace emotional or absolute statements with evidence-based claims.
Edit carefully for grammar, spelling, and formal tone.

You have a strong idea and a meaningful topic. With clearer structure and more precise academic language, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Here is a revision report to help strengthen your essay.

Overall evaluation
Your essay has a clear position: the airplane has changed people’s lives in major ways. You also organize your ideas into clear areas—travel, business, entertainment, and international relations—which gives the essay a good structure. The reader can easily understand your main argument.

To improve the essay, focus on three main areas:
thesis and argument development
grammar and word choice
formal academic style

Strengths
Clear topic: You stay focused on the effects of the airplane.
Logical organization: Each body paragraph discusses one area of impact.
Relevant examples: You use examples such as travel time, business, concerts, and war.
Personal engagement: Your voice is present, which makes the essay interesting.

Main revision priorities

A. Make the thesis stronger
Your introduction gives your opinion, but the thesis can be more precise and academic. Right now, the first paragraph includes several ideas, but they are not combined into one strong claim.

A stronger thesis could be:

“The invention of the airplane has transformed modern life by making travel faster, expanding international business and entertainment, and reshaping political relations between nations.”

This version is clear, direct, and previews your body paragraphs.

B. Develop ideas more fully
Some paragraphs make a good point but need deeper explanation.

For example:
In the business paragraph, explain *how* airplanes help business: face-to-face meetings, faster shipping of people and ideas, global partnerships, conferences, etc
In the entertainment paragraph, the Michael Jackson example is understandable, but one example is not enough. Explain the broader effect: world tours, sports leagues, film promotion, cultural exchange.
In the international relations paragraph, the positive and negative sides are important, but the ideas need more careful development and clearer transitions.

Try to follow this pattern in each body paragraph:
topic sentence
explanation
example
concluding sentence

C. Use a more formal academic style
Several expressions are too informal for an academic essay.

Examples:
“in my opinion” → unnecessary if the whole essay is argumentative
“I’m going to touch on” → too conversational
“just gave a concert” → too informal and time-specific
“&” → do not use ampersands in formal writing; write and

Also avoid parentheses when possible:
“solely (vacation) travel”
“intercontinental relations (politics)”

Instead, write the ideas directly in the sentence.

Language corrections

Word choice / spelling
There are several spelling and vocabulary errors:
efficeintly → efficiently
effected → usually affected
shorten → shortened
choosen → chosen
possibly → possible
musicans → musicians
perspective countries → respective countries
Pearl Habor → Pearl Harbor

Grammar
A few sentence-level grammar issues reduce clarity.

Examples:
“Travel times have been significantly shorten”  
  → “Travel times have been significantly shortened.”

“countries can work together much easier than previously possibly”  
  → “countries can work together much more easily than was previously possible.”

“these would not take place as often as the do”  
  → “these would not take place as often as they do”

“it was brought about some negative changes”  
  → “it has also brought about some negative changes”

Punctuation
You often use commas incorrectly or too often. Also, some sentences are run-on sentences.

For example:
“The invention of the airplane has also affected areas other than solely (vacation) travel, business, entertainment, & intercontinental relations (politics) have also felt the impact of the airplane.”

This is too long and unclear. You can divide it:
“The invention of the airplane has affected more than vacation travel. Business, entertainment, and international politics have also felt its impact.”

Paragraph-level suggestions

Introduction
Start with a general statement about the airplane’s importance.
End with a clear thesis listing the main effects.

Travel paragraph
Good example with Michigan to California.
Correct the flight time if necessary; make sure examples are realistic.
Add one sentence explaining why shorter travel matters socially or economically.

Business paragraph
Good main idea.
Replace personal observation (“I have seen this...”) with a more general statement unless the assignment encourages personal experience.

Entertainment paragraph
Good topic, but make the evidence broader and less dependent on one celebrity example.

International relations paragraph
This is your most complex paragraph and could become your strongest one.
Separate positive and negative effects more clearly.
Be careful with historical examples and spelling.

Suggested revised conclusion
Your conclusion should restate the main argument more clearly. For example:

“In conclusion, the airplane has had a profound impact on modern society. It has reduced travel time, strengthened global business and entertainment, and changed the way nations interact. Although airplanes have also been used in war, their overall influence on human life has been enormous.”

Final advice
When revising, focus first on:
rewriting the thesis
improving sentence clarity
correcting grammar and spelling
making the style more formal

You already have a solid structure and a strong central idea. With clearer language and more polished development, this can become a strong argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear main idea: AIDS changed people’s lives not only medically, but also socially and psychologically. You also use relevant examples, such as Ryan White, to support your argument. This gives your essay a strong foundation. For revision, focus on three main areas: accuracy of ideas, organization, and language control.

Strengths
Clear topic: You stay focused on how AIDS changed society.
Relevant examples: The Ryan White example is effective because it shows fear and discrimination in a concrete way.
Argumentative purpose: You are not only describing AIDS; you are arguing that it changed attitudes, behavior, and relationships.

Most important revision: improve accuracy and precision
Some claims in the essay are too broad or factually questionable. In argumentative writing, strong arguments depend on careful wording.

For example:
“it always leads to death and there is no cure”  
  This is too absolute. Today, treatment has changed outcomes greatly. Even if you are writing from an earlier historical perspective, avoid “always.”  
  Better: “For many years, AIDS was widely seen as a fatal disease, and the lack of a cure created fear.”

“there is more of stigma attached to being a homosexual male... but they probably have AIDS too”  
  This sentence risks repeating prejudice rather than analyzing it.  
  Better: “AIDS intensified stigma against gay men, as many people unfairly assumed they were infected.”

“casual sex... was a widely accepted idea, now it is something people really take into consideration and rarely occurs”  
  “Rarely occurs” is too sweeping and difficult to prove.  
  Better: “Public fear of AIDS made many people more cautious about casual sex.”

Try using words like many, some, often, in some cases, widely perceived, contributed to instead of absolute statements.

Organization and paragraph development
Your essay has good ideas, but the structure can be clearer. Right now, some points are mixed together. A stronger structure would be:

Introduction
Introduce AIDS as a major discovery/crisis that changed public thinking.
End with a thesis statement.

Example thesis:
“The emergence of AIDS significantly changed society by creating fear and prejudice, while also forcing people to rethink sexual behavior and public health.”

Body Paragraph 1: Fear and prejudice
Topic sentence about social fear and discrimination.
Explain stigma against gay men.
Use Ryan White as a specific example.

Body Paragraph 2: Changes in behavior and thinking
Topic sentence about how AIDS changed attitudes toward sex and personal responsibility.
Discuss caution, education, and awareness.

Conclusion
Restate the main argument.
End with a broader comment about how disease can transform society.

Language and grammar issues
There are several grammar, spelling, and punctuation errors. Below are common problems from your essay:

A. Spelling
Correct these:
peoples → people’s
awhile → a while
it’s presence → its presence
untill → until
devistating → devastating
definately → definitely
strands → possibly strains

B. Apostrophes
Be careful with its vs. it’s:
it’s = it is
its = possessive

So:
its presence
its consequences

C. Sentence structure
Some sentences are awkward or run together. Example:

Original:
“Also, when Ryan White, a boy who contracted AIDS through a blood transfusion was diagnosed, the people of his town and his school treated him like a complete outcast and wouldn't allow him to attend school.”

Improved:
“Similarly, when Ryan White, a boy who contracted AIDS through a blood transfusion, was diagnosed, people in his town treated him as an outcast and would not allow him to attend school.”

D. Word choice
“discovery” is not the best word for AIDS itself. AIDS is a disease/epidemic; what was “discovered” was the disease or its impact.
“undetectable strands” is unclear. You may mean undetectable strains or cases that remain undetected.

Style suggestions
To sound more academic:
Avoid “I feel” unless your teacher asks for personal opinion.
Use more formal transitions:
  - Furthermore
  - For example
  - In addition
  - As a result
  - Overall

Instead of:
“I feel this has really made people think about their actions.”  
Write:
“This crisis forced many people to reconsider their behavior and personal responsibility.”

Possible revised opening
Here is one way to rewrite your introduction:

One of the most significant public health crises to change people’s lives and ways of thinking was the emergence of AIDS. Although the disease may have existed earlier, it was not widely recognized until fairly recently, and its effects on society were devastating. The AIDS epidemic changed society by creating fear and prejudice, while also encouraging people to think more carefully about health, sexuality, and personal responsibility.

Final advice
Your essay has a strong topic and good supporting examples, but it will improve most if you:
make your claims more precise,
organize each paragraph around one main point,
correct grammar and spelling errors,
use a more formal academic tone.

You already have the basis of a persuasive essay; revision will make your ideas much clearer and stronger.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0015.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a logical overall structure: you begin with the discovery of penicillin, move to later developments in antibiotic synthesis, and end with the broader impact on medicine and industry. This shows good control of the essay’s timeline. Your vocabulary is also strong in places, especially with terms such as “antibiotic,” “synthesized,” “biologically active,” and “research teams.”

However, to revise this into a stronger argumentative essay, you should improve thesis focus, accuracy, sentence style, and grammar.

Clarify the argument
At the moment, the essay mostly describes the history and impact of penicillin. An argumentative essay needs a clearer claim that the reader can debate.

For example, your main argument could be something like:
The discovery of penicillin transformed modern medicine by improving survival rates, encouraging the development of synthetic antibiotics, and creating a major pharmaceutical industry.

This would help your body paragraphs support one central point. Right now, the essay sometimes feels like a short history report rather than an argument.

Improve paragraph unity
Each paragraph should have one main purpose.

Paragraph 1: Discovery of penicillin and its immediate importance.
Paragraph 2: How penicillin led to synthetic antibiotics and industrial growth.
Paragraph 3: Overall significance and continuing influence.

This structure is already visible, but you can make it stronger by adding a topic sentence to each paragraph that directly supports your thesis.

For example, paragraph 2 could begin:
After the supply of naturally occurring antibiotics became limited, chemists turned to synthetic methods, which expanded both medical treatment and the pharmaceutical industry.

Be careful with factual accuracy
A few statements are too broad or inaccurate:

“It was now much easier to obtain antibiotics for simple illnesses, such as the common cold.”  
  This is problematic because the common cold is caused by a virus, and antibiotics do not treat viral infections. You should remove or revise this point.

“thousands of universities and companies around the world continue the work of a small bacteria”  
  Penicillin is produced by a mold (Penicillium), not a bacterium. Also, “continue the work” sounds informal and unclear.

A more accurate revision:
Today, universities and pharmaceutical companies continue to study antibiotic production and develop new drugs based on the principles first demonstrated by penicillin.

Reduce repetition and improve style
You repeat words such as “discovered,” “synthesized,” and “research.” Repetition can make the essay sound mechanical.

Examples:
“It was discovered…”
“New drugs were also synthesized…”
“research teams devoted to producing efficient synthesis routes…”

Try using more active and varied wording:
Scientists found…
Chemists developed…
Researchers focused on…

Also, many sentences use passive voice. Passive voice is not always wrong in scientific writing, but too much of it makes the essay less direct.

Example:
“It was discovered that the older drugs could be synthesized efficiently…”
Better:
Researchers discovered that older drugs could be synthesized efficiently…

Grammar and word choice corrections
Here are some specific language issues to revise:

“medicinal chemistry” → better: “medicine” or “medical science” in this context.
“As discoveries of new antibiotics decreased” → awkward. Better:  
  “As the discovery of new antibiotics slowed…”
“It was also attemped” → spelling: “attempted”
“the increase of health of millions of people” → unnatural. Better:  
  “the improved health of millions of people”
“research of their uses” → better:  
  “research on their uses”
“a small bacteria” → “a small bacterium” if singular, but here the larger issue is that penicillin comes from mold, not bacteria.

Strengthen the conclusion
Your conclusion summarizes the essay, but it can be more forceful by restating the argument in a more polished way.

Possible revision:
In conclusion, the discovery of penicillin changed modern medicine in lasting ways. It not only saved millions of lives but also encouraged the development of new antibiotics and helped build the modern pharmaceutical industry.

Suggested revision priorities
When you revise, focus on these steps:

Write a clear thesis statement in the introduction.
Make sure each paragraph supports that thesis.
Correct factual errors, especially about the common cold and bacteria/mold.
Replace repetitive passive structures with clearer active sentences.
Edit grammar, spelling, and word choice.

Final comment
This essay shows good ideas and a strong sense of historical development. Your main challenge is to turn the piece from a general explanation into a more focused argument. With a clearer thesis, more precise facts, and smoother sentence style, your essay will become much more effective.

Your essay has a clear topic and a strong overall position: you argue that television is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed entertainment, communication, and family life. What you do well

   - Your main points are easy to identify: entertainment, communication, and family values.

Logical organization
   - The introduction presents the topic.
   - The body discusses different effects of television.
   - The conclusion restates the argument effectively.

Balanced discussion
   - You do not only praise television; you also mention its harmful effects.
   - This makes the essay more thoughtful and persuasive.

Main areas to improve

Strengthen your thesis

Current idea:
“This invention not only introduced a new form of entertainment, it has enhanced communication and has altered family life and values.”

This version is more direct and academic.

For example:

At times, your discussion jumps quickly from one idea to another. Add transitions such as:

Therefore

Use a more formal academic style

“yonkies”

“it is anyone’s guess”

overly dependent on television
rapid and accurate communication
it remains uncertain

Also, avoid using & in formal writing. Write and instead.

Correct grammar and word choice
There are many language errors, but most are fixable. Pay special attention to:

a. Confusing words
their / there / they’re
  - “their have been” → there have been
  - “their is” → there is

b. Noun forms
“20 century” → 20th century
“way of live” → way of life
“programing” → programming

d. Prepositions and spelling
“come in to our homes” → come into our homes
“half way” → halfway

Example corrections

Feedback Report

You have a clear argumentative focus: modern inventions like the washing machine make life easier, but they also reduce physical activity and may contribute to unhealthy lifestyles. This is an interesting idea, and your essay has an engaging, lively tone. Below are the main areas to improve in revision.

Strengthen your thesis
Your main argument appears only gradually. In an argumentative essay, the thesis should be clear and specific, usually at the end of the introduction.

At the moment, your introduction mainly gives background. Try to end it with a sentence such as:

Possible thesis:  
*Although the washing machine has made daily life more convenient, it has also encouraged physical inactivity and dependence on technology.*

This makes your position clear from the beginning.

Improve organization
Your essay has one main idea, but the structure could be clearer. Right now, some paragraphs repeat similar points about convenience and panic without developing them fully.

A stronger structure could be:

Introduction – background + thesis  
Body paragraph 1 – how washing machines save time and labor  
Body paragraph 2 – how people have become dependent on modern devices  
Body paragraph 3 – how reduced manual work may affect health  
Conclusion – restate argument and give final thought

Each body paragraph should begin with a topic sentence that connects to your thesis.

Example:  
*One major benefit of washing machines is that they save people a significant amount of time and effort.*

Use a more formal academic tone
Your voice is interesting and humorous, but some parts sound too exaggerated or conversational for an academic essay.

Examples:
*millions and millions of lives were made simpler* → too dramatic
*furiously running about in the streets, clutching the machine tightly to their chests* → funny, but unrealistic
*What ever would a college student do...* → rhetorical and informal
*But is that all that bad?....* → conversational punctuation

In academic writing, try to use measured claims instead of exaggeration.

For example:
*For many households, the washing machine has become an essential appliance.*
*Many people would find it difficult to return to washing clothes by hand.*

Develop your argument with evidence and explanation
Your essay makes a claim that machines reduce exercise and may contribute to weight problems, but this idea needs more support. Right now, it relies mostly on opinion.

To improve, explain:
Why washing machines reduce physical effort
How labor-saving devices may affect daily activity
Whether convenience outweighs the disadvantages

You do not need detailed research in every sentence, but you should add more reasoning. For example:

*Because household appliances reduce the need for manual labor, people burn fewer calories during daily tasks. As a result, modern convenience may contribute to a more sedentary lifestyle.*

Also, be careful not to oversimplify. For example, *“was weight watchers ever an issue?”* is too broad and historically weak. People in the past also had health problems, just different ones.

Avoid overgeneralization
Some statements are too broad:
*For as long as man has been alive...*
*The mere thought of washing clothes by hand causes panic in most teenagers, students, & adults alike.*
*many people might be seen... clutching the machine tightly...*

These are not accurate for everyone. Use softer language:
*many people*
*in some households*
*for many adults*
*people often seem reluctant to...*

This makes your argument more believable.

Grammar, spelling, and word choice
There are several language issues to revise.

Spelling / word form
continously → continuously
necesity → necessity
with out → without
What ever → Whatever
handclothes washing → washing clothes by hand or hand-washing clothes

Style and usage
Avoid “man” to mean all humanity; it sounds outdated. Use people, humans, or society.
Avoid & in formal writing. Use and.
Avoid too many quotation marks and dots: “modern” society, ....
*in those houses that own them* → in households that own one

Pronouns
*tell him/her* is understandable, but in formal modern English, them is smoother:
  - *Give an average adult a washboard and soap and tell them to do a load of laundry...*

Conclusion
Your final sentence is memorable, but the conclusion should more clearly summarize your argument. Instead of ending with only a rhetorical question, state your final position directly.

Example:
*In conclusion, the washing machine has greatly improved daily life by saving time and effort. However, like many modern inventions, it has also reduced physical activity and increased our dependence on technology. Society should enjoy these benefits while remaining aware of their effects on health and lifestyle.*

Overall
This essay has a clear central idea, strong energy, and a good attempt at argument. Your next step is to make it more academic, better organized, and more precise. Focus especially on:
a clearer thesis,
stronger paragraph structure,
more formal language,
fewer exaggerations,
careful correction of grammar and spelling.

You have a good foundation here; with revision, this could become a much stronger argumentative essay.

Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay is lively, personal, and easy to follow. You clearly like your topic, and that enthusiasm makes the writing engaging. The examples are practical and memorable, especially the story about the roast beef sandwich and the suitcase with shampoo. These details help persuade the reader that the zip-lock bag is useful. Your organization is also mostly clear: you introduce your opinion, give reasons, add examples, and end with a conclusion.

To revise, focus on three main areas: academic tone, grammar/word choice, and argument structure.

Academic tone
Your opening is friendly and natural, but it sounds more like conversation than formal argumentative writing:

“It is funny that we are writing on this subject…”
“I’m going to have to agree…”

In an academic essay, try to begin more directly with your claim. For example:

Possible revision:
> One of the most useful inventions in everyday life is the zip-lock bag because it preserves food, contains odors, and serves many practical purposes beyond food storage.

This kind of thesis is clearer and more formal.

Also, some expressions are too casual:
“What more could you ask for?”
“A third solution would be to bag everything!”

These are amusing, but in an argumentative essay, a more formal style is usually stronger.

Grammar, article use, and word choice
There are several small language errors. Here are the most important ones:

a. Singular/plural and articles
“Anything can be put in zip-lock bag”  
  → “Anything can be put in a zip-lock bag”
“Another thing that zip-lock bags are good for is keeping certain odors in the bags and certain odors out.”  
  This is understandable, but awkward. Better:
  → “Another advantage of zip-lock bags is that they keep odors contained and prevent outside smells from affecting the contents.”

b. Prepositions and phrasing
“to bring to school to lunch”  
  → “to bring to school for lunch”
“foods to which they are near”  
  → “foods that are nearby” or “foods stored near them”

c. Punctuation
There are a few punctuation problems:
“my locker. . Finally” → remove the extra period
“Gallon size zip-lock bags, solve this problem.”  
  → no comma: “Gallon-size zip-lock bags solve this problem.”
“too wet to pack ,” → remove the space before the comma
The dash in “dry-clean only’s” is fine, but “only’s” is not correct. Better:
  → “dry-clean-only clothes” or “dry-clean-only items”

d. Spelling
“earing” → “earring”
“pantyhose you do not want to snag, the earing…”  
  This list needs parallel structure and probably plurals:
  → “pantyhose you do not want to snag, earrings you do not want to lose, and shaving cream you do not want to spill”

Strengthen the argument structure
Your essay has many good examples, but sometimes it reads like a list of uses rather than a fully developed argument. To improve it, make sure each body paragraph has:
a clear topic sentence,
explanation,
an example,
a link back to your thesis.

For example, your essay could be organized like this:

Paragraph 1: Zip-lock bags preserve food freshness.
Paragraph 2: They control odors and prevent contamination from other smells.
Paragraph 3: They have many non-food uses, especially for travel and storage.

This will make the essay feel more focused.

Be careful with overstatement
You write, “that is an invention that I cannot live without!” This is expressive, but maybe too exaggerated unless you prove it more strongly. In academic argument, it is often better to say:
> It is one of the most useful everyday inventions.

This sounds more reasonable and persuasive.

Conclusion
Your conclusion restates the main idea well, but it could be slightly stronger by summarizing your reasons more clearly.

Possible revision:
> In conclusion, the zip-lock bag is an important invention because it keeps food fresh, prevents unwanted odors, and has many practical uses in daily life and travel. Its convenience, light weight, and versatility make it an especially valuable household item.

Final advice
Your biggest strengths are:
clear enthusiasm
strong everyday examples
easy-to-follow overall structure

For revision, work on:
making the tone more academic
correcting grammar and spelling errors
organizing each paragraph around one clear reason
replacing casual phrases with more precise language

You already have a strong idea and good supporting examples. With cleaner grammar and a more formal style, this essay could become much more effective.

Strong sense of importance
You successfully communicate that computers have changed both daily life and work. Phrases like *“forever changed the way people live and work”* and *“The impact of computers on the world has been great”* show confidence and purpose.

Main areas to improve

Right now, many claims are broad. For example, you say computers changed business and increased productivity “ten-fold,” but you do not explain how. In an argumentative essay, readers need concrete support.

students and researchers can access information quickly online

This will make the essay easier to follow.

“Much has occured” → “Much has occurred”

“As we move into the 2st century” → “As we move into the 21st century”

“We are limited only by our imaginations”

Quotation marks around “bright”

Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay has a clear purpose: you argue that AIDS changed society’s attitudes toward sexuality, mortality, and public health, and that education and compassion are more important than fear and discrimination. This is a strong and meaningful topic, and your essay shows engagement with the social impact of AIDS. “How wrong they were.”

effect → affect

“a twelve year old hemophiliac” → “a twelve-year-old hemophiliac”

writing a clearer thesis,
improving paragraph organization,
using formal academic language,
correcting grammar and punctuation,
and supporting broad claims more carefully.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear personal voice and a strong central position: you argue that medical inventions are the only twentieth-century inventions that have truly improved human life. This is an interesting and ambitious argument. Your writing also shows good abstract thinking; you are not only listing inventions, but trying to explain the human motives behind invention itself. That gives your essay depth.

To improve the essay, focus on clarity, support, and language accuracy.

Strengths

Strong thesis
Your main claim is clear:
> “the only inventions of the twentieth century that have significantly changed people's lives for the better are those that have occured in the medical field.”

This gives the essay a definite direction.

Thought-provoking ideas
Your discussion of fear, insecurity, death, and domination is original. It shows critical thinking, which is valuable in argumentative writing.

Coherent contrast
You create a contrast between:
most inventions = harmful / controlling
medical inventions = life-saving / kind

This contrast helps organize your argument.

Main areas to improve

A. Add specific evidence and examples
The biggest weakness is that your essay stays too general. You make very broad claims, but you do not support them with concrete examples.

For instance, when you say medical inventions improve life, name some:
antibiotics
vaccines
X-rays
insulin
MRI or CT scans

Then explain how they changed people’s lives.

Likewise, when you criticize other inventions, give examples:
nuclear weapons
industrial machinery that damages the environment
surveillance technology
certain transportation systems that increase pollution

Without examples, the essay sounds more like a philosophical reflection than a persuasive argument.

B. Make your argument more balanced
Some of your claims are too absolute, which weakens the essay. For example:
> “Medical inventions… cannot be used to dominate nature or other people.”

This is difficult to defend. Medical technology can also be connected to power, inequality, or unethical use. A stronger version would be:
“Medical inventions are primarily intended to save lives and reduce suffering.”
“Compared with many other inventions, medical technologies have brought more direct benefits to humanity.”

This kind of wording sounds more reasonable and persuasive.

C. Improve organization
Your essay has a thesis and supporting paragraphs, but the development is limited. A clearer structure would be:

Introduction: present topic and thesis more directly  
Body paragraph 1: explain why many inventions have harmed nature  
Body paragraph 2: explain how inventions are used to dominate other people  
Body paragraph 3: explain why medical inventions are different, with examples  
Conclusion: restate thesis and summarize main points

Right now, the essay ends very quickly. Add a concluding sentence or paragraph to leave the reader with a strong final impression.

Style and tone

Be careful with overly emotional or sweeping language
Some phrases are powerful, but too strong for an academic argument:
“obsessed with technology”
“petty insecurities”
“destroying ourselves”
“one of the only truely ‘kind’ expressions”

These expressions show feeling, but in academic writing, it is better to sound precise and controlled.

For example:
Instead of “obsessed with technology,” write “highly dependent on technological development.”
Instead of “petty insecurities,” write “social and psychological insecurities.”

Language corrections

Here are some important grammar and word-choice corrections:

occured → occurred
affect (noun not needed here) → effect in “a negative effect on our lives”
truely → truly
0the century → 20th century or twentieth century
Remove extra spaces before punctuation:  
  - “For these reasons ,” → “For these reasons,”

Sentence-level suggestions

Original:
> “Interestingly enough, this topic is one that is very important to me.”

More academic:
> “This topic is particularly important to me.”

Original:
> “By closely analyzing the nature of modern inventions, one notes a consistent trend leading away from a lifestyle that is peacefully coexistent with nature.”

Better:
> “A close analysis of modern inventions reveals a trend away from living in harmony with nature.”

Original:
> “We continue to destroy the plants, animals, and the earth itself and our new inventions help us to do it in more efficient ways.”

Better:
> “We continue to damage plants, animals, and the earth itself, and new inventions often make this destruction more efficient.”

Revision priorities

When you revise, do these three things first:

Add at least 2–3 concrete examples of medical inventions and explain their positive effects.
Add examples of harmful non-medical inventions to support your criticism.
Use more moderate, academic language so your argument sounds credible.

Final comment

You have a promising argument and a distinctive voice. Your essay is most successful when it connects technology to larger human values. To make it stronger, support your ideas with evidence and reduce overgeneralizations. With clearer examples and a more academic tone, this could become a very persuasive essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and an ambitious goal: you want to argue that the satellite dish has transformed communication, politics, business, and entertainment. That is a strong and interesting idea. You also try to consider both positive and negative effects, which is good for argumentative writing. To improve the essay, focus on clarity of argument, organization, evidence, and language accuracy.

Main argument and thesis

At the moment, your main claim is not fully clear. In the introduction, you suggest that the satellite dish changed the world, but in the conclusion you say it is up to the reader to decide whether the change is good or bad. An argumentative essay should usually take a more definite position.

Suggestion: write a thesis that clearly states your opinion.

For example:
“The satellite dish has transformed modern life by accelerating global communication, influencing politics and business, and reshaping entertainment, although its effects are not always positive.”

This thesis is stronger because it:
makes a clear claim,
previews the main body points,
allows you to discuss both benefits and drawbacks.

Organization

Your paragraphs mention several different ideas, but the essay sometimes feels like a list rather than a developed argument. Try to organize each paragraph around one main point.

A possible structure:

Introduction  
   - background on satellite dishes  
   - clear thesis

Politics and news  
   - CNN and global news  
   - how leaders use fast information

Business and economics  
   - global markets, communication, trade  
   - how companies benefit

Entertainment and culture  
   - MTV, global media, celebrity economy

Negative effects  
   - overdependence on television/media  
   - possible misuse in military or political systems

Conclusion  
   - restate thesis  
   - summarize major effects

Also, make sure each paragraph has:
a topic sentence
supporting explanation/examples
a concluding sentence or transition

Development and evidence

Many of your claims are interesting, but some are too general or unsupported. For example:
“Decisions... are being made, based on that nights news CNN commentator.”
“40% of U.S. millionares are entertainers.”
“The intelligence of an average teenager is lowering itself due to cable viewing...”

These statements need evidence. In academic writing, readers need facts, statistics from reliable sources, or careful explanation. If you cannot prove a claim, make it more cautious.

For example:
Instead of “The intelligence of an average teenager is lowering itself”, write  
  “Some critics argue that excessive television viewing may reduce the time teenagers spend reading or studying.”

This sounds more academic and reasonable.

Also, some examples are not fully connected to your argument. For instance, “STARWARS” and “naval programs” are mentioned very briefly, so the reader does not understand their role. Either explain them clearly or remove them.

Style and tone

Your essay has energy, but some phrases are too informal, exaggerated, or confusing for academic writing.

Examples:
“as large as a small FIAT” — vivid, but not necessary in formal writing
“dictators alike switch the channel” — too conversational
“The only observation which can be made” — too absolute

Try to use a more formal, precise style.

For example, instead of:
“has changed the size of the world”
write:
“has made the world more interconnected”

That expresses the idea more clearly.

Grammar and word choice

There are several language issues that you should revise carefully.

Common problems in this essay:

A. Apostrophes / possessives
it’s → its  
  (“it’s” = “it is”; “its” shows possession)

B. Capitalization
panasonic → Panasonic
The Satellite dish → usually the satellite dish
Avoid unnecessary capitals such as The Information Age unless it is part of a proper title.

C. Spelling
recieve → receive
bases → basis
affect / effect:  
  “have had a strong effect on...”
millionares → millionaires
pro’s → pros
people lives → people’s lives

D. Sentence structure
Some sentences are awkward or incomplete. For example:
“Not certain about the date of its invention...”  
  This is a fragment.
“Stocks are an integral part of their livelihood.”  
  This does not clearly connect to the previous sentence.

Try to write shorter, clearer sentences.

Example of revision

Original:
> “The creation of the satellite dish in the past 20 years, has altered the average intelligence of the human being for the better and sometimes worse.”

Improved:
> “Over the past few decades, the satellite dish has influenced how people receive information, bringing both educational benefits and possible negative effects.”

This version is clearer, more formal, and easier to support.

Final advice

For revision, focus on these priorities:

Write a clear thesis with a definite position.
Reorganize the essay into clear body paragraphs with one idea each.
Support claims with specific examples and avoid unsupported generalizations.
Use more formal academic language.
Correct grammar, spelling, capitalization, and punctuation.

You have a strong central idea and good instincts about the broad impact of technology. With clearer structure and more careful language, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and an interesting central claim: television has changed people’s lives through shared culture, commercialization, and faster access to information. This is a strong argumentative structure because you present three main effects and then conclude with a warning about television’s power. Your voice is confident, and several examples are vivid, especially your discussion of family sitcoms, advertising, and war coverage.

What is working well

Clear thesis and organization
Your introduction states the main idea and previews the body paragraphs. This helps the reader follow your argument.

Strong topic focus in body paragraphs
Each body paragraph develops one effect of television:
mass culture
commercialization
speed and amount of information

This is a good academic pattern.

Engaging examples
You use concrete references such as *The Cleavers*, *Green Acres*, the Challenger explosion, and the Vietnam War. These examples make the essay more persuasive and memorable.

---

Main areas to revise

Correct the title and opening
“The 0th Century” is incorrect. It should be “20th Century.” Also, the first sentence should be smoother and more precise.

Original:  
“The 0th Century Invention that has had one of the most radical effects on people's lives is the television.”

Better:  
“One of the twentieth century inventions that has had the most radical effect on people’s lives is television.”

Note also that in formal writing, we usually say “television” rather than “the television” when speaking generally.

---

Use a more formal academic style
Your essay has good ideas, but some expressions are too informal for academic writing.

Avoid:
& → use and
w/ and w/o → use with and without
conversational phrases like “of course”
sentence fragments and dramatic punctuation

Example:
Original:  
“The television a powerful and dangerous tool.”

Corrected:  
“Television is a powerful and dangerous tool.”

Try to keep a consistent formal tone throughout the essay.

---

Improve grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that distract from your argument. Focus especially on these:

Spelling / word form
grupes → groups
breath of information → breadth of information
lead to → led to
peoples ability → people’s ability
livingrooms → living rooms
vietnam war → Vietnam War
inhance → enhance
united States → United States

Awkward or unclear wording
“mass culturization” is not natural English. Better choices:
  - mass culture
  - cultural homogenization
  - cultural standardization

“the commercialization of our lives” is understandable, but “the commercialization of everyday life” sounds more academic.

---

Strengthen paragraph development
Your body paragraphs have good main ideas, but some points need more explanation.

Paragraph 1: Shared culture
This is your strongest paragraph in concept. However, be careful with broad claims such as:
“To white people, Ghetto life is slightly less of a mystery.”

This could sound oversimplified or insensitive. Try to express the point more carefully:
“Television has exposed many viewers to social groups and lifestyles outside their own experience, although these representations are often stereotyped or exaggerated.”

This keeps your meaning but makes it more precise and academic.

Paragraph 2: Commercialization
This paragraph is currently too short. You mention that advertising influences emotions and consumption, but you should explain how this affects people’s lives. For example:
Does it encourage consumerism?
Does it shape desires and values?
Does it turn entertainment into a vehicle for marketing?

Add 2–3 more sentences of explanation or an example.

Paragraph 3: Information and reaction
This paragraph has strong ideas, especially about live coverage and war. However, some sentences are too long and should be divided for clarity.

Original:  
“Today, we know within seconds, or in the case of the space-shuttle challenger explosion, we know live-immediately.”

Better:  
“Today, news reaches us within seconds. In some cases, such as the Space Shuttle Challenger explosion, events are broadcast live.”

This is clearer and more grammatical.

---

Make your conclusion more polished
Your conclusion has a strong final message, but it needs grammatical correction and a more formal finish.

Original:  
“we are open to manipulation as well as stimulation.”

This is effective. You can build on it.

Possible revision:  
“Overall, television has radically changed modern life. It informs and entertains us, but it also shapes our values, desires, and understanding of reality. For this reason, viewers must remain aware of its power to influence what they think and how they live.”

---

Suggested revision priorities
Correct grammar, spelling, and punctuation errors.
Replace informal abbreviations with formal academic language.
Expand the paragraph on commercialization.
Clarify and soften overgeneralized claims.
Revise the introduction and conclusion for polish and accuracy.

---

Final comment
This essay shows strong critical thinking and a clear argument. Your main task now is to make the language more formal and precise so that your ideas sound as strong as they really are. You already have a solid structure; with careful editing and fuller development in a few places, this could become a very effective argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a strong emotional opening and a clear sense of historical importance. You write with passion, and that gives the piece energy. The personal memory of watching events unfold and the anecdote about your classmate’s father are especially effective because they make a large historical event feel human and real. Your main argument is also clear: the fall of the Berlin Wall was a catalyst in the end of the Cold War.

To improve the essay, focus on clarity of argument, organization, grammar, and word choice.

Strengths

Strong opening
The first paragraph immediately creates drama and captures the reader’s attention. This is a good strategy for argumentative writing, especially when the topic is historical.

Personal engagement
Your personal connection to the event makes the essay more vivid. The memory of school reactions and the story of the Hungarian father add emotional depth.

Clear central claim
By the end, your position is understandable: the fall of the Berlin Wall played a major role in ending the Cold War.

---

Main areas to revise

A. Make the thesis more precise
Right now, your argument appears gradually, but it should be stated more directly near the end of the introduction.

For example, you could write:
“Although the collapse of communism had many causes, the fall of the Berlin Wall was the key catalyst that accelerated the end of the Cold War by inspiring resistance across Eastern Europe.”

This gives the essay a stronger argumentative focus.

---

B. Improve paragraph structure
At the moment, the essay reads somewhat like a reflection mixed with an argument. That is not wrong, but for an argumentative essay, each body paragraph should develop one clear point.

A possible structure:

Introduction  
   Emotional opening + historical context + thesis

Body Paragraph 1: Symbolic importance of the Wall  
   Explain why the Wall represented division, oppression, and the Cold War itself.

Body Paragraph 2: Impact on Eastern Europe  
   Show how events in Berlin encouraged people in other Eastern Bloc countries.

Body Paragraph 3: Why it helped end the Cold War  
   Explain how the Wall’s fall weakened communist control and changed global politics.

Conclusion  
   Restate argument and explain why the event is still remembered.

This structure will help your reader follow your reasoning more easily.

---

C. Distinguish between emotion and evidence
Your essay is persuasive emotionally, but it needs a little more factual support. For example, instead of saying only that countries “rose against communist regimes,” briefly explain what happened and why it matters.

You do not need many details, but one or two specific examples would strengthen the argument:
democratic changes in Poland
protests in Czechoslovakia
the collapse of regimes across Eastern Europe

These examples would make your claim more convincing.

---

D. Correct grammar and awkward phrasing
There are several language errors that make the essay less polished. Here are important examples:

“mesmirized” → “mesmerized”
“one of those historic moment” → “one of those historic moments”
“the start of what our now as-President Bush likes to refer to as”  
  This is awkward. Better: “the start of what President Bush now refers to as”
“Tchekoslovaquia” → “Czechoslovakia”
“Roumania” → modern spelling: “Romania”
“Without the ‘events of Berlin Wall’” → “Without the events surrounding the Berlin Wall” or “Without the fall of the Berlin Wall”
“history probably would not have taken a very different course”  
  This is confusing because of the double negative. Better:  
  “history probably would not have taken a very different course” if that is your meaning, or more clearly:  
  “history might still have moved in the same direction eventually.”
“the catalyst force” → “the catalytic force” or simply “the catalyst”

Also, be careful with pronouns:
You write “I had a classmate whose father had escaped from Hungary… her father’s words…”  
  This is possible, but make sure the reader clearly understands that the classmate is female.

---

E. Improve punctuation
You often use dashes (“--”) where periods or commas would be better. Too many dashes make the essay feel informal and can confuse the reader.

For example:
“The world watched in anticipation -- We were mesmerized…”  
  could become  
  “The world watched in anticipation. We were mesmerized…”

Try to use:
periods for complete thoughts
commas for shorter pauses
dashes only occasionally for emphasis

Also correct the final sentence ending:
“the day the wall fell --” should end with a period, not dashes.

---

Style suggestions

Be careful with repetition
You repeat “It was…” several times in the second paragraph. Repetition can be powerful, but too much weakens the effect. Try varying sentence openings.

Use a more formal tone in some places
Phrases like “Have you heard?” work well in the introduction because they recreate a moment, but the rest of the essay should sound more academic. Try to balance personal voice with analytical language.

---

Final advice
Your essay already has a compelling voice and a meaningful argument. The next step is to make it more academic and controlled: state your thesis clearly, organize each paragraph around one idea, add a little more evidence, and revise grammar and punctuation carefully.

You have the basis of a strong essay. With revision, it can become both moving and persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that television was the most important invention of the twentieth century because it changed how people receive information, entertainment, and connection to the world. This is a strong, focused claim, and your writing shows ambition in vocabulary and tone. You also do a good job of comparing television with radio, which gives your argument a logical structure.

What is working well

Clear argumentative focus
From the introduction, your reader understands your main idea: among many inventions, television had the greatest impact. This is important in an argumentative essay.

Effective comparison
Your discussion of radio before television is useful because it helps show what television added. The contrast between “hearing” and “seeing” is one of the strongest parts of the essay.

Strong sense of significance
You do not simply describe television; you explain why it matters. Your points about global communication, politics, sports, education, and persuasion show that you are thinking beyond entertainment.

---

Main areas to revise

Make the thesis more precise
Your thesis currently suggests that television “stands out,” but it could be more direct and argumentative. Try to state exactly why television was the most important invention.

For example:
> Of all twentieth-century inventions, television had the greatest impact because it transformed how people received news, experienced entertainment, and connected with events around the world.

This version tells the reader the three main points that the essay will discuss.

---

Improve paragraph structure
Some paragraphs contain interesting ideas, but they need clearer topic sentences and more development.

A possible structure:
Introduction + thesis
Life before television: newspapers and radio
How television improved communication through images
How television connected people globally
The power and dangers of television
Conclusion

This would make your argument easier to follow.

---

Develop your evidence
Many of your points are interesting, but they remain general. Add more specific examples to make your argument stronger.

For instance:
Instead of saying television brought the world together, mention televised events such as wars, political speeches, moon landing coverage, or the Olympics.
When you say television can be useful in medicine, politics, and education, explain how.

Specific examples make your argument more convincing.

---

Watch word choice and phrasing
There are several vocabulary and usage errors that make the essay harder to read. Here are some important corrections:

“one lives easier” → “our lives easier” or “one’s life easier”
“advancing a civilization” → “advancing civilization”
“vertually” → “virtually”
“and or” → “and/or” or better “a radio or a newspaper”
“boarders” → “borders”
“peak” → “peek” (but “glimpse” is better here)
“absorbtion” → “absorption”
“the worlds together” → “the world together”
“olympic” → “Olympic”
“spening” → “spending”
“television strength” → “television’s strength” or “the power of television”
“necesary” → “necessary”

Also, some expressions sound unnatural in academic writing, such as:
“all ends of its borders” → “across the country”
“higher level of enjoyment television watching may bring” → “greater enjoyment than radio may provide”

---

Correct grammar and punctuation
There are many sentence-level issues, especially with commas, apostrophes, and sentence fragments.

Examples:

“Before the late 1940's”  
  Better: “Before the late 1940s”  
  (No apostrophe needed for decades.)

“A system that seemed to work, many households…”  
  This is a fragment/comma splice. Better:  
  “This system seemed to work because many households owned or had access to a radio or newspaper.”

“pictures that enhanced the entire absorbtion of what was being broadcast”  
  Better:  
  “pictures that improved people’s understanding of what was being broadcast.”

Try reading each sentence aloud. If it feels too long or confusing, divide it into two shorter sentences.

---

Strengthen the conclusion
Your final sentence, “keep the vision in television,” is memorable, but the conclusion should first clearly restate your argument. Then you can finish with a strong phrase.

For example:
> Television became one of the most influential inventions of the twentieth century because it changed not only how people were entertained, but also how they understood the world. Although it can be used to persuade and manipulate, it remains a powerful tool when used responsibly.

---

Final advice
For revision, focus on three priorities:
Write a sharper thesis.
Organize each paragraph around one main idea.
Edit carefully for grammar, spelling, and sentence clarity.

You have a strong central idea and several thoughtful points. With clearer structure and more precise language, this essay could become much more persuasive and polished.
