Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay has a clear main idea: the computer is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed how people and businesses handle information. This is a strong topic, and your writing shows good awareness of how computers affect society. You also organize your essay logically, moving from the past, to the development of the personal computer, to its effects on the future.

To improve the essay, focus on thesis clarity, paragraph unity, grammar, and style.

Strengths

Clear topic
You clearly identify the computer as a major invention and explain its importance.

Logical structure
Your essay follows a reasonable progression:
introduction of the topic,
background on early computers,
development of the personal computer,
social impact and future importance.

Good range of ideas
You mention several useful effects of computers: writing, storing information, communication, portability, and access to databases.

Main Areas to Revise

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction suggests the topic, but the thesis could be sharper and more direct. Right now, the first sentence is broad, and the final sentence of the introduction is long and slightly awkward.

Current idea:  
“The computer… has had a great impact on how society and especially business gathers, creates, responds to and, understands everyday information.”

Possible revision:  
“Among the inventions of the twentieth century, the computer has had the greatest impact because it transformed how people and businesses store, process, and share information.”

This version is clearer and easier to argue.

B. Strengthen the argumentative focus
Your essay is more explanatory than argumentative. You explain what computers do, but you should more directly prove why the computer is more important than other inventions.

To make the essay more argumentative, add points such as:
computers increased speed and efficiency,
computers changed both work and private life,
computers made access to information much easier,
computers became the foundation for later technologies such as the internet and mobile devices.

Try to make each body paragraph support one clear reason.

C. Improve paragraph unity
Some paragraphs are very long and include many different ideas. For example, the third paragraph discusses:
IBM,
the personal computer,
home use,
software,
modems,
portable computers,
fax and phone technology.

This is too much for one paragraph. Split it into two shorter paragraphs:
the invention and spread of the personal computer
how later developments increased its usefulness

This will make your essay easier to read.

Grammar and Sentence-Level Issues

A. Subject-verb agreement
“There has been many inventions” → “There have been many inventions”

B. Article use
“with the use of Modem” → “with the use of a modem”
“through the phone on the other computers” → better: “through telephone lines to other computers”

C. Word choice / form
“recieve” → “receive”
“everyday information” → probably “daily information” or “everyday tasks involving information”
“in an easier fashion” → “more easily”

D. Capitalization
“Modem” should usually be “modem”
“Second World War” is acceptable, but be consistent with style.

E. Awkward or unclear phrasing
Some sentences are too long and difficult to follow. For example:

Original:  
“It has made our lives easier in turn, because with more information you need a tool to better sort it out and put that information into a way we can more easily process it.”

Revision:  
“It has made our lives easier because, as the amount of information increases, we need tools that can organize it and present it in a form we can process more easily.”

Try to write shorter, clearer sentences.

Style Suggestions

Avoid repetition
You repeat “information” many times. Since it is central to your topic, some repetition is fine, but too much makes the style heavy. You can vary it with words like:
data
knowledge
records
materials
content

Use more formal academic style
Some phrases sound too informal:
“just about anything”
“tap into”
“do anything from almost anywhere”

Replace them with more academic alternatives:
“a wide range of tasks”
“gain access to”
“work remotely in many situations”

Suggested Revision Plan

Rewrite the introduction with a stronger thesis.
Divide the long third paragraph into two focused paragraphs.
Make each body paragraph support one main reason why computers are important.
Shorten long sentences and improve clarity.
Correct grammar, spelling, and article errors.
Strengthen the conclusion by restating the main argument more confidently.

Example of a Stronger Concluding Sentence

“For these reasons, the computer can be considered one of the most influential inventions of the twentieth century, since it revolutionized communication, work, and access to information.”

Final Comment

You have a solid foundation and a meaningful topic. Your main challenge is not ideas, but control: clearer argument, better paragraphing, and more accurate grammar. With revision, this could become a strong argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear personal voice and an ambitious idea: that the 20th century can be called “The Century of Democracy.” This is a strong argumentative claim, and your references to the Berlin Wall, the Soviet Union, and the end of the Cold War show that you are trying to support your argument with historical examples. The essay is engaging and sincere. For revision, the main goals are to make the argument more focused, more precise, and better supported.

Strengths

Strong central idea: The claim that democracy is the defining feature of the 20th century is interesting and debatable.
Good sense of audience: Your opening tries to attract the reader’s attention with familiar images from different decades.
Personal engagement: The essay feels authentic, especially when you mention your own feelings and experiences.
Relevant historical examples: Germany, the Soviet Union, and the Cold War are all useful examples for this topic.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more direct
Your thesis appears at the end of the first paragraph: “I think it is only fair to call the 20th Century, ‘The Century of Democracy.’”  
This is a good start, but it should be more specific. *Why* is democracy the defining feature? Because it spread to more countries? Because it ended dictatorships? Because it reduced war?

A stronger thesis might be:

> The 20th century should be called “The Century of Democracy” because democratic movements helped reunify divided nations, weaken communist regimes, and encourage more peaceful international relations.

This version tells the reader exactly what your body paragraphs will discuss.

B. Reduce the long introduction
The first paragraph spends a lot of time discussing earlier decades (“Roaring 20s,” “Great Depression,” “Fabulous Fifties”). This is interesting, but it delays your main point. In an argumentative essay, the introduction should quickly lead to the thesis.

Try shortening the background and moving faster to your claim.

C. Strengthen the argument with clearer evidence
Right now, several ideas are broad or generalized:
“the world today… is becoming a better place”
“people all over the world finally take a stand”
“The idea of a nuclear war is practically non-existent today”

These statements need more explanation or evidence. For example:
What specific democratic movements are you referring to?
Why did German reunification represent democracy?
In what ways did the end of the Cold War create more peaceful relations?

Also, be careful not to overstate. Saying nuclear war is “practically non-existent” may sound too absolute.

D. Improve historical accuracy and precision
A few phrases are inaccurate or imprecise:
“the invention of the automobile” — the automobile was not invented in the 1920s.
“East and West Germany to unite as one democratic superpower” — Germany reunited, but it is not usually called a “superpower.”
“rid their nation of its communist government” — the Soviet transition was much more complex than this phrase suggests.
“the former superpower of the Soviet Union is now making its transition into a democracy” — this was a complicated and unstable process, so be careful with simplified wording.

Using more precise historical language will make your argument more credible.

Organization
Your body paragraphs mostly follow a logical order, but the essay would be stronger if each paragraph had:
a clear topic sentence,
one main point,
supporting explanation,
a link back to the thesis.

For example:
Paragraph 1: Democracy and German reunification
Paragraph 2: Democratic change in the Soviet Union
Paragraph 3: Democracy’s effect on global peace after the Cold War

Style and tone
Your style is expressive, but sometimes it becomes too emotional or conversational for an academic argument:
“How could we ever forget…?”
“No, I’m not engulfed…”
“I feel lucky…”

Personal voice is welcome, but in argumentative writing, it should support the analysis rather than replace it. Try to keep a more formal tone.

Language corrections
Here are some sentence-level issues to revise:

“The ‘Roaring 20’s’” → “The ‘Roaring Twenties’”  
“the 30’s” → “the 1930s”
“20th Century” → usually “20th century” (no capital letters unless part of a title)
“Perhaps, it is because…” → no comma after “Perhaps”
“The Soviet Union, as well is going…” → “The Soviet Union as well is going…” or better “The Soviet Union is also undergoing…”
“marxism” → “Marxism”
“more peaceful relations” → “more peaceful international relations”
“if we all have one bond- democracy-” → “if we all share one bond—democracy—”

Also watch article use and word choice. For example:
“could follow its happening” is awkward. Better: “could witness this historic change.”

Final advice for revision
When you rewrite, focus on these priorities:

State a sharper thesis
Shorten the introduction
Use 2–3 clear historical examples
Explain how each example proves the importance of democracy
Use a more formal academic tone
Correct inaccurate or vague historical claims

Overall
This essay shows good ideas and strong enthusiasm, which are excellent foundations for argument writing. Your next step is to turn that passion into a more focused, precise, and evidence-based essay. With revision, your central claim could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear strong opinion and an urgent tone, which works well for argumentative writing. You clearly want to persuade the reader that nuclear energy is dangerous and that society should act now. The ending also gives the reader a call to action, which is effective. Below are some suggestions to help you revise and make your argument stronger, clearer, and more academic.

Main strengths
Clear position: You strongly argue against nuclear energy.
Passionate voice: The essay shows conviction and concern.
Logical general structure: You begin with the invention of atomic energy, explain why it is feared, discuss its modern use, and end with a warning and recommendation.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your main claim is understandable, but it should be stated more directly and specifically in the introduction. Right now, the essay moves from the atomic bomb to nuclear energy in general, but the argument is not fully focused.

For example, your thesis could be something like:
“Although nuclear energy can provide electricity, its environmental and human risks are too great, so governments should reduce and eventually end its use.”

This kind of thesis clearly tells the reader:
there is an opposing side,
your position,
your recommendation.

B. Improve accuracy and development of ideas
Some claims are too broad or scientifically unclear. For example:
“blowing holes in our sky”
“holes in our ozone layer” as a result of nuclear use
“rendering our seas useless”

These phrases are dramatic, but they need either evidence or more accurate explanation. In an academic argument, strong emotional language is less persuasive than clear facts and examples.

You could strengthen your essay by including specific evidence such as:
nuclear accidents (Chernobyl, Fukushima)
radioactive waste problems
health risks from radiation exposure
costs of plant maintenance and cleanup
comparison with renewable energy alternatives

C. Organize body paragraphs more clearly
Each paragraph should have one main idea. Right now, some paragraphs mix several ideas together. A stronger structure might be:

Introduction + thesis
Paragraph 1: destructive power of nuclear weapons
Paragraph 2: dangers of nuclear energy production
Paragraph 3: why some countries still use it
Paragraph 4: why alternatives are better
Conclusion: restate argument and call for action

This would help your essay feel more controlled and persuasive.

Language and style corrections

A. Word choice
Some vocabulary choices are not natural or are slightly incorrect:
“routed in” → “rooted in”
“augments” → “increases”
“power source alternatives” → “alternative energy sources”
“lack of sight” → “lack of foresight”
“irreversable” → “irreversible”

B. Grammar and sentence form
There are several sentence-level issues with punctuation, capitalization, and incomplete phrasing.

Examples:
“The discovery of the Atomic energy, is undoubtedly the the most awesome…”
  - Remove unnecessary comma
  - “Atomic energy” should not be capitalized
  - delete repeated “the”

  Better:
  “The discovery of atomic energy is undoubtedly one of the most awe-inspiring as well as terrifying inventions of the twentieth century.”

“For not only does it have the capacity to kill, but also the capability to supply and energize the world.”
  - This is awkward in structure.

  Better:
  “It not only has the capacity to kill, but also the ability to supply energy to the world.”

“what is to become of our children.”
  - This should be a question:
  “What is to become of our children?”

C. Tone
Your essay is passionate, but sometimes the tone becomes too emotional for academic writing. Phrases like:
“the saddest part of this tragedy”
“It starts with you”
“Write your local congressman”

are powerful in persuasive speech, but in an academic essay, it is usually better to sound more formal and balanced.

For example, instead of:
“It starts with you. Write your local congressman.”

You could write:
“Citizens should pressure their elected representatives to support safer and more sustainable energy policies.”

Content suggestion: acknowledge the other side
A strong argumentative essay often becomes more convincing when it briefly recognizes the opposing view. You mention that nuclear energy can “supply and energize the world,” but you do not fully develop that point.

You could add a sentence such as:
“Supporters of nuclear energy argue that it produces large amounts of electricity with lower carbon emissions than fossil fuels; however, the risks of accidents, waste disposal, and long-term contamination still make it too dangerous.”

This makes your argument sound more thoughtful and mature.

Final revision priorities
When you revise, focus on these steps:

Write a clearer thesis
Use specific evidence and examples
Separate each body paragraph by one main point
Replace dramatic but vague phrases with precise language
Correct grammar, spelling, punctuation, and capitalization
Use a more formal academic tone

Overall
This essay shows strong engagement with the topic and a real desire to persuade the reader. Your next step is to make the argument more precise, evidence-based, and academically structured. With clearer organization and more careful language, this could become a much more convincing argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that television is one of the most significant inventions of the 20th century because it changed how people receive information and understand the world. This is a strong and appropriate topic for an argumentative essay. You also include both advantages and disadvantages, which makes your writing more balanced.

Strengths

Clear main idea
   - From the introduction, your opinion is easy to identify.
   - The essay stays focused on television rather than moving to unrelated topics.

Relevant supporting points
   - You explain that television allows people to see events as they happen.
   - Your example of war coverage is effective because it shows a real social impact.
   - The idea that television has “shrunk the planet” is strong and memorable.

Formal tone
   - Your vocabulary is generally academic and suitable for this kind of essay.

---

Main Areas to Improve

Make the thesis more precise
Your thesis is understandable, but it could be more direct and specific. Right now, the introduction repeats general ideas about communication before arriving at the main claim.

For example, instead of:
*“If I were to choose the most frequently used and influential invention of the field, it would have to be the television.”*

You could write:
“Among modern communication technologies, television is one of the most influential inventions of the 20th century because it delivers immediate visual information, shapes public opinion, and connects people to global events.”

This version clearly previews your main arguments.

---

Improve paragraph organization
Each body paragraph should begin with a clear topic sentence and then develop one main point.

For example:

Paragraph 1: Television changed public awareness by showing real events visually.
Paragraph 2: Television made the world feel smaller and became a powerful tool of influence.
Paragraph 3 (optional): Television also has negative effects, such as desensitization or misuse.

At the moment, some ideas are mixed together. For instance, the second body paragraph discusses global awareness, power, harm, and desensitization all at once. Try to separate ideas more clearly.

---

Use stronger argumentative development
Some claims need more explanation or evidence. For example:
*“This simple fact has changed in many ways how we live…”*
*“There are many who feel that it can be a harmful thing…”*

These are interesting points, but they remain general. Ask yourself:
How has television changed people’s lives?
Why is it harmful?
What examples prove this?

Adding one or two specific examples to each paragraph will make your argument more convincing.

---

Correct grammar and sentence structure
There are several grammar issues, especially with tense, articles, capitalization, and awkward phrasing.

a. Verb tense
Because you are discussing the present influence of television, use present tense consistently unless referring to historical events.

Examples:
*“television had changed people's lives”* → “television has changed people’s lives”
*“like all inventions can be used”* → “like all inventions, it can be used”

b. Capitalization
Proper nouns and names must be capitalized:
*“the vietnam war”* → “the Vietnam War”

c. Word choice / natural phrasing
Some expressions sound unnatural in English:
*“has to do with the speed at which information is now communicated”*  
  → “is the speed with which information can now be communicated”
*“made smaller the dimensions of the planet”*  
  → “made the world seem smaller”
*“unknown and unheard of areas”*  
  → “places that were once little known”

d. Sentence clarity
Some sentences are too long or awkward. For example:
*“There are, however, several downsides to the television, and, like all inventions can be used in a useless way.”*

Better:
“However, television also has several disadvantages and, like all inventions, it can be used poorly.”

---

Strengthen the conclusion
Your conclusion repeats your main point, which is good, but it could end more powerfully. Try to summarize your reasons and leave the reader with a final thought.

For example:
“Although television can be misused and may desensitize viewers, its ability to inform, influence public opinion, and connect distant parts of the world makes it one of the most important inventions of the 20th century.”

---

Suggested Revision Priorities

Rewrite the thesis so it clearly lists your main reasons.
Reorganize body paragraphs so each one develops one central idea.
Add more specific explanation or examples.
Edit grammar carefully, especially tense, punctuation, and capitalization.
Make the conclusion more concise and forceful.

---

Final Comment

This essay shows strong potential. Your ideas are thoughtful, and you clearly understand the broad social impact of television. The main revision goal is clarity: make your argument more structured, more specific, and grammatically smoother. If you revise with those points in mind, your essay will become much more persuasive.

Feedback on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay has a clear main idea: the airplane is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed travel, cultural understanding, and international relations. This is a strong topic, and your writing shows genuine thought and personal engagement. Your example about visiting Spain is especially effective because it makes your argument more personal and believable.

What you do well

Clear position
From the first sentence, your reader understands your opinion: the airplane significantly changed people’s lives. This is important in an argumentative essay.

Good organization of ideas
Your essay moves logically:
introduction of the invention
effects on travel and cultural understanding
effects on international relations and communication

This structure helps the reader follow your argument.

Effective personal example
Your discussion of your own experience in Spain is one of the strongest parts of the essay. It supports your point that travel broadens people’s perspectives.

---

Main areas to improve

Strengthen the thesis
Your introduction gives the topic, but your thesis could be more precise. Instead of saying the airplane changed lives “astronomically,” explain how it changed them.

For example:
> The invention of the airplane has significantly changed people’s lives by making long-distance travel faster, increasing cultural understanding, and improving international communication and trade.

This gives your essay a clearer roadmap.

---

Develop each body paragraph more fully
Your body paragraphs have good ideas, but some points need more explanation or evidence.

For example, in paragraph 2 you say people were more ethnocentric before air travel. This is an interesting argument, but you should explain it more carefully. How exactly does travel reduce ethnocentrism? What happens when people meet others directly?

In paragraph 3, your points about politics, business, and postal communication are promising, but they are too brief. Try to expand each one with a concrete example.

For instance:
political leaders can meet quickly in times of crisis
companies can transport goods and workers internationally
airmail made communication faster across continents

---

Use a more formal academic style
Some phrases are too informal or imprecise for an academic essay.

Examples:
“opened doors” → acceptable, but repeated too often
“things like disease” → too informal
“basically speeded up” → too conversational
“whole postal communication system also” → awkward phrasing

Try to use more formal vocabulary and precise wording.

---

Improve grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that affect clarity. Here are some important corrections:

Word choice
“The extend to which” → “The extent to which”
“long distance traveling” → “long-distance travel”
“time consuming” → “time-consuming”
“witness it first hand” → “witness them firsthand” or “experience them firsthand”
“rapid paced” → “fast-paced”

Grammar and sentence structure
“It also introduced what seemed a completely impossible concept…”  
  This sentence is awkward. Consider:
  > It also made possible what had once seemed impossible: human flight.

“One could travel by boat but this was inconvenient…”  
  Add a comma before “but”:
  > One could travel by boat, but this was inconvenient…

“The frequency that which foreign countries are able to communicate…”  
  This phrase is not correct. You could write:
  > The frequency with which representatives of different countries can communicate and negotiate face to face has increased dramatically.

“has improved drastically the establishment of international trade”  
  Better:
  > This has greatly improved the development of international trade.

“speeded up”  
  Better academic choice:
  > accelerated

---

Add a conclusion
Your essay ends suddenly. A conclusion is necessary to give the essay a finished shape. In your conclusion, briefly restate your main argument and summarize the airplane’s importance.

For example:
> In conclusion, the invention of the airplane transformed modern life by making travel faster, exposing people to new cultures, and strengthening global political and economic connections. For these reasons, it remains one of the most influential inventions of the 20th century.

---

Suggested revision strategy

When revising, try these steps:

Rewrite your thesis so it clearly lists your main reasons.
Expand each body paragraph with one or two specific examples.
Improve formal style by replacing informal expressions.
Correct grammar and phrasing sentence by sentence.
Add a short conclusion.

---

Final comment

This essay has a solid foundation and a meaningful argument. Your ideas are interesting, and your personal example adds strength to your writing. The next step is to make your argument more precise, more formal, and more fully developed. With revision, this could become a strong academic essay.

Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay addresses an important topic and has a clear purpose: to argue that education is essential in preventing AIDS, especially when medicine alone is not enough. This is a strong central idea, and your concern for public health is clear throughout the essay. You also try to persuade the reader by giving examples and showing the seriousness of the issue. That is a good foundation.

What is working well

Clear topic and purpose  
   Your essay stays focused on AIDS awareness and prevention. The reader can understand that your main argument is that education may help prevent the disease.

Strong sense of urgency  
   You communicate that this issue is serious and should not be ignored. This helps make the essay persuasive.

Use of examples  
   You include examples such as school assemblies, drug use, and risky sexual behavior. These examples support your main point that knowledge can influence behavior.

Main areas to improve

Make your thesis more precise
Your essay suggests the main idea, but it would be stronger if you stated it clearly in one sentence near the beginning.

For example:  
Although medical science has made great progress, education remains one of the most effective ways to prevent the spread of AIDS.

This gives the reader a clear roadmap for the essay.

Improve organization
Your essay has ideas, but the paragraphs do not always develop them in a logical order. Try this structure:

Introduction: introduce the topic and thesis
Body Paragraph 1: limits of medicine in fighting AIDS
Body Paragraph 2: importance of public education
Body Paragraph 3: examples of risky behavior and why awareness matters
Conclusion: restate the main argument and end with a strong final thought

Also, make sure each paragraph has one main idea.

Use a more formal academic style
Some expressions are too informal for an academic essay. Avoid:

& → use and
w/ → use with
contractions like it’s in formal writing when possible
emotional or dramatic phrases like “killing our population” or “possible suicide”, unless carefully explained

Academic writing should sound serious, clear, and precise.

Be careful with accuracy and tone
A few parts of the essay may sound misleading or overly general. For example:

“homosexuals are not the only possible carriers”  
  This can sound stigmatizing. AIDS is not limited to one group. It is better to focus on behaviors rather than specific identities.

“dentists who forget to put on their plastic gloves”  
  This example sounds too casual and not fully accurate. If you mention medical transmission, explain it carefully and factually.

A better approach would be:  
Anyone can be at risk if they engage in unsafe practices such as unprotected sex or sharing contaminated needles.

Sentence grammar and word choice
There are many grammar issues that make some sentences difficult to read. Pay special attention to:

verb tense consistency
subject-verb agreement
articles (a, an, the)
plural forms
word choice
punctuation

Specific language corrections

Here are some examples from your essay:

“The medical profession of the 20th century has made many exciting discoveries advancing its ability to cure many ailing human beings”  
  Better: Medical science in the twentieth century has made many important discoveries and improved its ability to treat disease.

“If AIDS can not be prevented by medication”  
  Better: If AIDS cannot be prevented by medication alone

“people choose to ignore that it exists; however, it does & it's killing our population”  
  Better: many people choose to ignore its existence; however, the disease continues to affect communities around the world.

“they interact sexual w/ complete strangers”  
  Better: they engage in sexual activity with complete strangers

“The AIDS viruses was discovered”  
  Better: The AIDS virus was discovered

“changed many peoples lives for the worst”  
  Better: changed many people’s lives for the worse

Suggestions for revision

When you revise, focus on these priorities:

Write a clear thesis statement.
Reorganize the essay so each paragraph has one main idea.
Replace informal abbreviations with full academic language.
Use neutral, accurate wording to avoid stereotypes.
Edit carefully for grammar, punctuation, and sentence clarity.

Possible revised thesis and concluding idea

Possible thesis:  
Medical advances have helped society fight many diseases, but education is still one of the most important tools for preventing the spread of AIDS.

Possible conclusion idea:  
By increasing public awareness about transmission and prevention, society can reduce fear, correct misinformation, and help protect future generations.

Final comment

You have chosen a meaningful topic and you clearly want to persuade the reader. Your main strength is your strong message about the importance of education. Now you should work on clarity, organization, grammar, and academic tone. With revision, your essay can become much more effective and convincing.

Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay is lively, personal, and easy to follow. You clearly show why contact lenses matter to you, and your examples about sports, swimming, and waking up able to see are strong because they make the topic concrete. Your voice is confident and engaging, which is a real strength.

That said, if this is meant to be an argumentative essay, you need to revise it so it sounds more formal, more focused, and more argumentative rather than mainly personal and conversational.

What you do well

Clear position
   - Your main opinion is obvious: contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century.

Good use of personal experience
   - Your experiences help explain the practical benefits of contact lenses.

Logical overall structure
   - You move from introduction, to older lenses, to soft lenses, to disposable lenses, and then to your conclusion.

Main areas to improve

Make the essay more argumentative
Right now, the essay reads more like a personal reflection than an academic argument. An argumentative essay should present a claim and support it with broader reasons.

Your current claim:
contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century

To support this better, you should include arguments such as:
they improve quality of life
they allow greater freedom in sports and daily activities
they are more convenient than glasses for many users
they have developed over time to become safer and more comfortable

You could also briefly acknowledge another view, for example:
although glasses are cheaper or easier for some people, contact lenses offer greater mobility and convenience

This would make your essay more balanced and persuasive.

Use a more formal academic tone
Your essay often uses spoken, informal language, which is interesting but not suitable for formal academic writing.

Examples:
“I can’t even begin to explain...”
“a pain to clean”
“lense craze”
“who am I to say!”
“glasses are a thing of the past!”

These expressions sound conversational. Replace them with more neutral alternatives.

For example:
“Disposable lenses offer significant advantages.”
“Hard lenses were often considered uncomfortable and difficult to maintain.”
“Their popularity increased rapidly.”

Also, avoid using:
& → use and
too many exclamation marks
rhetorical questions in excess

Improve grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that you should correct.

Common issues:
a. Subject-verb agreement
“a style called ‘hard lenses’ were the only kind made”
Correct: “a style called ‘hard lenses’ was the only kind made”

b. Spelling
“lense” → lens
“lense case” → lens case

c. Prepositions and phrasing
“my only form of seeing better” → my only way of seeing clearly
“made accessible to the public” → made available to the public

d. Awkward or unclear wording
“If glasses were my only form of seeing better, I’d be blind most of the time!”
Better: If glasses were my only option, I would be unable to see clearly during many daily activities.

Strengthen paragraph unity
Some paragraphs mix different ideas. Try to make each paragraph focus on one main point.

A possible structure:
Introduction: thesis about why contact lenses are an important invention
Body paragraph 1: freedom in sports and active life
Body paragraph 2: comfort and improvement from hard to soft lenses
Body paragraph 3: convenience of disposable lenses
Body paragraph 4: possible counterargument and response
Conclusion: restate argument clearly

Revise punctuation and formatting
There are many punctuation problems:
extra spaces
unusual semicolons
unnecessary quotation marks
a hyphen used incorrectly
sentence fragments or run-on style

For example:
“Also, I enjoyed swimming- even though...”
  - Better: “I also enjoyed swimming, even though...”
“But, people buy them, so who am I to say!”
  - Better: “However, many people purchase them.”

Quotation marks should only be used when necessary. Words like hard lenses and soft lenses usually do not need quotation marks.

Example of a stronger thesis
Instead of:
“Without a doubt, one of the most important inventions of the 20th century has been contact lenses.”

You could write:
Contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because they improve vision while offering comfort, convenience, and greater freedom in everyday and athletic activities.

This thesis is more specific and gives a clear plan for the essay.

Final advice for revision
When revising, focus on these priorities:
Make your essay sound less like a personal story and more like an academic argument.
Replace informal expressions with formal language.
Correct grammar, spelling, and punctuation.
Build each paragraph around one clear supporting reason.
Add a counterargument to strengthen your persuasion.

You have a strong foundation because your enthusiasm is genuine and your examples are memorable. With a more formal style and clearer argument structure, this could become a convincing argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear central idea: feminism can be understood as an important “discovery” of the twentieth century because it changed how society thinks about women’s roles, rights, and image. This is an interesting and original argument. The essay is also organized logically, moving from a general introduction to examples about work, politics, and social attitudes. To revise it, focus mainly on accuracy, development of ideas, and language precision.

Strengths
Interesting thesis: Calling feminism a “discovery” is creative and gives your essay a clear focus.
Clear structure: Introduction, body points, and conclusion are easy to follow.
Relevant themes: You discuss equality at home, in politics, in pay, and in cultural representation.

Main revision priorities

A. Make your historical details more accurate
Some facts in the essay are inaccurate or too general, which weakens your argument.

Examples:
“the 0th century” should be “the 20th century.”
Women in the U.S. did not win the right to vote in the 1800s; nationally, this happened in 1920.
The Equal Rights Amendment was proposed earlier and became a major issue in the 1970s, but it was not the first significant equal rights movement in the U.S.
“60 cents for every dollar” may be outdated or unsupported unless you provide a source.

Advice: If you mention historical examples, be careful and specific. Even in a short essay, accuracy matters.

B. Develop your argument with clearer evidence
Right now, some claims are interesting but underexplained. For example:
Why is feminism a “discovery” rather than simply a movement?
How exactly did it change men’s and women’s lives?
In what ways do pornography and language shape the image of women?

Try to add one or two specific examples for each body paragraph. This will make your essay more persuasive.

For example:
In the paragraph about equality, mention voting rights, access to education, employment opportunities, or legal protections.
In the paragraph about women’s image, explain how media or everyday language can reinforce stereotypes.

C. Strengthen the thesis and topic sentences
Your thesis is promising, but it could be more direct. At the moment, the introduction compares feminism with inventions like cars and telephones, but the exact argument could be sharper.

A stronger thesis might say:
> Although feminism is not a technological invention, it can be seen as one of the twentieth century’s most important social discoveries because it revealed inequality, challenged traditional gender roles, and transformed public attitudes toward women.

Then each body paragraph can connect clearly to one part of this thesis.

Language and style corrections

Word choice
Some phrases sound unnatural or slightly incorrect in English:
“modern day life” → modern life
“towards the equality of women” → toward equality for women
“it was still the most typical as well as the ideal” → it was still considered typical and ideal
“housekeeping and tending the children” → doing housework and taking care of the children
“helps to form” → help form
“breaking it down” (referring to image/stereotype) → challenging it or dismantling it

Grammar
A few sentences need grammatical revision:
“how much things as varying as pornography or the language we use helps”  
  → how much things as varied as pornography and the language we use help
“Only when women have achieved equality with men, will there cease to be a need for feminism”  
  → Only when women have achieved equality with men will there cease to be a need for feminism.  
  (No comma needed.)

Punctuation and formatting
Avoid extra spaces after periods.
Quotation marks around “discovery” and “discovered” are acceptable, but using them too often can weaken your confidence. If this is your main concept, explain it instead of putting it in quotation marks repeatedly.

Organization suggestions
Your essay is coherent, but it would be stronger with a more standard argumentative structure:

Introduction
   - Introduce the idea of discoveries in the 20th century
   - Present feminism as a major social discovery
   - State your thesis clearly

Body Paragraph 1: Legal and political equality
   - voting rights
   - equal rights movements
   - laws and public change

Body Paragraph 2: Economic equality
   - women entering the workforce
   - wage gap
   - changing family roles

Body Paragraph 3: Cultural image of women
   - media, language, stereotypes
   - why changing ideas matters

Conclusion
   - Restate why feminism is an ongoing discovery
   - End with a strong final thought

Final advice
For revision, focus on these three questions:
Are my facts accurate?
Have I explained each point with enough detail?
Does each paragraph clearly support my thesis?

You already have a thoughtful central idea and a solid basic structure. With more precise evidence and cleaner language, this could become a strong argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and a consistent focus: you argue that concern for the environment is one of the most important ideas of the 20th century. What is working well

Clear main idea

Good range of examples

pollution and nuclear waste
natural resources
recycling
energy conservation
animal protection

This variety helps support your argument.

Logical paragraph organization

For example, in the introduction or conclusion, you could say that environmental awareness changed:
government laws
business practices
personal habits
international cooperation

This will make the essay more argumentative, not only informative.

Improve grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that make some sentences awkward or unclear. Here are important examples:

“countries that which have many weapons” → “countries that have many weapons”
“were never ending” → “were never-ending” or “would never run out”
“what we use, needs to be replaced” → “what we use needs to be replaced”
“people are more inclined to turn off their lights when they're not in their rooms” → correct, but could be smoother: “people are more likely to turn off lights when they leave a room”
“We realize that conserving is not beneficial to our environment but it is also saves money.”  
  → “We realize that conservation is not only beneficial to the environment, but it also saves money.”
“more considerable of our animal life” → “more considerate of animal life” or better “more concerned about animal life”
“hopeful by the 21st century” → “hopefully by the 21st century”

Use formal academic style
Some abbreviations and symbols are too informal for an academic essay:
& → and
w/o → without
b/c → because
2 → two

Also avoid putting informal comments in parentheses unless necessary. Try to integrate ideas directly into sentences.

Fix sentence structure and punctuation
A few sentences are run-ons or have comma problems. For example:

“For example deforestation is a very big issue, many groups have formed to plant more trees.”  
  This should be divided or connected correctly:  
  “For example, deforestation is a major issue, and many groups have formed to plant more trees.”

“Many countries realize that recycling is not only beneficial to their economy it also is beneficial to their environment.”  
  Add punctuation and improve parallel structure:  
  “Many countries realize that recycling is beneficial not only to the economy but also to the environment.”

Try reading each sentence aloud. If it sounds too long or rushed, it may need punctuation or division.

Use stronger topic sentences
Some paragraphs begin a little vaguely, such as “Another important topic we are all aware of is recycling.”  
Try more direct topic sentences, for example:
“Recycling has changed both consumer behavior and national environmental policy.”
“Energy conservation has influenced daily habits in homes and workplaces.”

This will make your writing sound more confident and academic.

To deepen the essay, you could add:
one or two specific examples of environmental laws or movements
clearer explanation of how environmental thinking changed ordinary life
acknowledgment that this idea is still developing, but already influential

“Although many problems remain, environmental awareness has pushed societies to take more responsibility for the future.”

---

For revision, focus on these priorities:
strengthen the argument about why this idea changed people’s lives
correct informal language
revise grammar and sentence structure
make the conclusion more realistic and polished

You have a solid foundation and a clear central idea. With more precise language and a stronger argumentative focus, this essay can become much more convincing.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear main idea: computers have had a major impact on people’s lives, and you support this idea with personal examples. The paper is easy to follow in general, and your examples about storing information, balancing a checkbook, editing documents, and traveling to Madrid are relevant. You already have good material; revision should focus on organization, grammar, word choice, and academic style.

Main strengths
Clear topic: You consistently discuss how computers make life easier.
Relevant examples: Personal experience makes your argument concrete and believable.
Good range of ideas: You mention storage, calculation, writing, communication, and travel systems.

Main areas to improve

A. Organization and thesis
Your essay needs a stronger introduction with a clear thesis statement. Right now, you begin with a personal statement, but the argument would be stronger if you clearly stated your position.

For example:
Possible thesis: *Computers have significantly improved people’s lives by making information storage, daily tasks, and communication more efficient.*

Then organize the essay into body paragraphs, each with one main point:
Computers help store and organize information.
Computers save time and reduce errors in daily tasks.
Computers make communication and travel arrangements easier.

Your second paragraph about Madrid is interesting, but it feels slightly disconnected. Make sure it clearly supports your main argument about computers improving modern life.

B. Academic style
Because this is an argumentative essay, your tone should be more formal. Avoid informal abbreviations such as:
& → *and*
w/ → *with*
w/out → *without*
info → *information*

Also, phrases like *“Well”* and *“no doubt”* sound conversational. In academic writing, use more formal transitions such as:
*Furthermore*
*In addition*
*For example*
*As a result*

C. Grammar and sentence structure
Several sentences are too long and contain many ideas at once. This creates run-on sentences and makes your writing harder to read. Try to divide long sentences into shorter ones.

Example from your essay:
*“I'm able to store almost all the information I need for school & for other things in my computer whereas before I had a computer my desk was cluttered...”*

This should be broken into two or three sentences.

Also check verb forms:
effected → *affected*
peoples lives → *people’s lives*
my life has change → *my life has changed*
had to this → *had to do this*
form the US → *from the US*
would inevitably inundated → *would inevitably be inundated*

D. Word choice
Some vocabulary choices are awkward or incorrect:
*“effected peoples lives”* → *affected people’s lives*
*“the power of computers, for me, is incomprehensible”* → this sounds too extreme. Better: *“the full potential of computers is still difficult for me to understand.”*
*“transaction would seem impossible minus the aid of computers”* → *“the transaction would seem almost impossible without computers.”*

E. Clarity and logic
Some claims are too absolute. For example:
*“Without the use of computers I would still be struggling...”*
*“the transaction would seem impossible...”*

These statements may sound exaggerated. In argumentative writing, it is often stronger to be precise rather than dramatic:
*“Without computers, the process would likely have been much slower and less efficient.”*

Sentence-level corrections
Here are a few examples of how you could revise sentences:

Original: *Computers have definitely effected peoples lives.*
  - Revised: *Computers have definitely affected people’s lives.*

Original: *I have a computer and my life has change because of it.*
  - Revised: *I have a computer, and my life has changed because of it.*

Original: *Of course, this was very time consuming & open to error.*
  - Revised: *This process was very time-consuming and often led to errors.*

Original: *I'm now here in Madrid. How did I get there?*
  - Revised: *My experience studying in Madrid also shows the importance of computers.*

Revision advice
When you rewrite, try this structure:

Introduction
General statement about computers
Clear thesis

Body Paragraph 1
Storage and organization of information
Example: school materials and records

Body Paragraph 2
Speed and accuracy in daily tasks
Example: balancing your checkbook and editing documents

Body Paragraph 3
Communication and modern services
Example: online ticket purchasing and study-abroad arrangements

Conclusion
Restate your thesis
Briefly explain why computers are so important in modern society

Final comment
You have strong ideas and useful examples, which is an excellent foundation. Your next step is to present these ideas in a more formal and organized way. Focus especially on clear paragraph structure, avoiding abbreviations, correcting grammar, and shortening long sentences. With revision, this essay can become much more persuasive and polished.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a strong central idea: satellites changed the world because they allow people to witness events immediately and globally. This is a good argumentative topic, and your example of the Tiananmen Square protests is powerful and relevant. You also show ambition in your language and try to create an emotional effect, which is a strength. To revise successfully, focus on clarity, organization, grammar, and word choice.

Main strengths
Clear position: You strongly argue that the satellite is a major invention.
Effective example: The China example helps support your claim.
Strong sense of importance: Your essay shows conviction and engages the reader.

Main problems to revise

A. Thesis and focus
Your first sentence says:  
> “Without question, the geo-centric satellite is the invention of the century.”

This is a strong opening, but your thesis should be more precise. “Invention of the century” is a very big claim. You need to explain why in one clear sentence.

A stronger thesis could be:
> The satellite is one of the most important inventions of the century because it makes global communication immediate, allows the world to witness history in real time, and increases public awareness of political events.

This gives your essay a clearer structure.

B. Organization
Right now, many sentences feel disconnected, and some are incomplete. Try this structure:

Introduction
   - Introduce the satellite
   - State your thesis

Body paragraph 1
   - Explain how satellites changed communication
   - Compare them with radio, TV, and videotape

Body paragraph 2
   - Use Tiananmen Square as evidence
   - Explain how live global broadcasting affected public opinion

Conclusion
   - Restate why satellites matter
   - End with a thoughtful final point

C. Sentence clarity
Some sentences are difficult to understand because of grammar or phrasing. For example:

> “As it happens- the most profound byproduct of the satellite.”

This is a sentence fragment. You may mean:
> The most profound benefit of the satellite is that events can be seen as they happen.

Another unclear sentence:
> “Freedom US. the tank of injustice made its way into the lives of most of the world's people.”

This sentence is not understandable in its current form. Perhaps you mean:
> Through satellite television, the image of the lone Chinese student standing before a tank reached people around the world.

Always ask: Can a reader understand exactly what I mean?

Grammar and language issues

A. Word choice
Several words are incorrect or not natural in English:
geo-centric satellite → probably communications satellite or geostationary satellite
evention → inventions
preceed → precede
openned → opened
litterally → literally
Teniman Square → Tiananmen Square
he young hero → the young hero
sensorship → censorship
a like → alike
mor → more

Also, avoid informal symbols in formal essays:
T.V. & videotape → television and videotape

B. Singular/plural and agreement
Examples:
none of these invention possess  
  → none of these inventions possesses / none of these inventions possess
the world functions  
  → the way the world functions

C. Punctuation
You often use commas, dashes, and periods incorrectly. Examples:
Avoid unnecessary commas after And, Certainly, However
Do not create sentence fragments with dashes
Keep complete thoughts in full sentences

For example:
> “And, to a certain extent, it has influenced now the world functions.”  
Better:
> To a certain extent, it has influenced the way the world functions.

Argumentation
Your argument becomes stronger when you explain how satellites create political impact. Right now, you suggest that satellites make governments “less tempted to do wrong.” That is interesting, but it needs support. Ask yourself:
Why does live coverage matter?
How does public attention influence governments?
Can global visibility encourage accountability?

Instead of making a very broad claim, explain it carefully:
> Because satellites allow live international coverage, governments know that their actions may be immediately seen and judged by the global public.

This is more academic and persuasive.

Tone and style
Some phrases are too emotional or informal for an academic essay:
“who wouldn't want to help his neighbor?”
“the satellite keeps a watchful eye on all of us”

These are vivid, but they sound more rhetorical than analytical. In academic writing, aim for a formal and controlled tone.

For example, instead of:
> Truth before censorship—this is the biggest advantage the satellite offers.

You could write:
> The greatest advantage of satellite communication is its ability to transmit information quickly and reduce governments’ ability to hide important events.

Suggested revision strategy
Rewrite your thesis clearly.
Divide the essay into introduction, body, and conclusion.
Correct spelling and grammar errors.
Replace unclear or dramatic phrases with precise academic language.
Expand your example of Tiananmen Square with clearer explanation.

Model revision of your opening
Here is one possible revision of the first part:

> Without question, the satellite is one of the most important inventions of the twentieth century. It has allowed people in distant parts of the world not only to hear about events, but also to see them as they happen. Although earlier inventions such as radio, television, and videotape made modern communication possible, none of them offered the same immediacy. By making live global communication possible, satellites have changed the way people understand world events.

You have a strong idea and a meaningful example. Now your main task is to express that idea in clearer, more accurate English and in a better-organized argument.

Feedback Report on Your Essay

Your essay has a clear topic and a positive, confident tone. You argue that computers have changed both workplaces and education, and your main idea is easy to understand. You also use examples from libraries, hospitals, law firms, and student writing, which helps support your argument. Strengths

Relevant examples: You mention companies, libraries, hospitals, law firms, and students.
Logical overall structure: Introduction, body paragraphs about business and students, and a conclusion.

Main Areas to Improve

A. Make your thesis stronger
Your introduction presents the topic, but the main argument could be sharper. Right now, you suggest that computers influence “people and companies,” but the essay would be stronger if you clearly stated how and in what areas.

For example, your thesis could say:
Computers have transformed modern life by improving information storage in businesses and creating new learning opportunities for students.

This kind of thesis prepares the reader for the two main body paragraphs.

B. Improve paragraph unity and development
Each paragraph should have one clear main idea and then explain it with examples.

For instance:
Paragraph 2: computers help businesses and institutions manage information efficiently.
Paragraph 3: computers make writing easier for students.
Paragraph 4: computers also support learning in other subjects.

Right now, some ideas are interesting but not fully explained. For example, “great teaching instruments” is a good point, but you should explain how computers help students learn biology or mathematics. Do they use simulations? visual graphs? educational software? Add one or two specific examples.

C. Use a more formal academic style
Some expressions are too informal for an academic essay:
“w/o” → without
“god send” → godsend (but this is still a bit informal; better: extremely useful tool)
“who knows where these machines will take us” → this sounds conversational. A more academic ending would be stronger.

definantly → definitely

  Better: With respect to ordinary people, computers have definitely had

  Better: much of it can now be stored

  Correct: They have enabled students

  Correct: for some students, they have been an incredibly useful tool
“but they is clearly imp”  
  Correct: but they are clearly important

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and several thoughtful ideas. You argue that television changed people’s lives by spreading information quickly, connecting cultures, and providing entertainment that can teach lessons. These are strong points for an argumentative essay. Below are suggestions to help you revise.

What is working well

Clear topic
You answer the prompt directly by choosing television and explaining why it is important.

Good range of ideas
You do more than list benefits. You explain:
fast access to news,
the ability to react quickly to events,
shared information across society,
cultural exchange,
educational value in entertainment,
some dangers of unrealistic TV content.

This makes your essay more interesting and balanced.

Thoughtful examples
Your references to world events, different countries, and entertainment help show the wide impact of television.

Main areas to improve

Strengthen the essay structure

Right now, the essay has many good ideas, but they are packed into very long sentences and paragraphs. This makes the argument harder to follow.

Try this structure:

Introduction
Introduce the invention.
State your main argument clearly.

Body Paragraph 1: Television and news
Television gives immediate access to events.
People can form opinions faster and respond more quickly.

Body Paragraph 2: Television and global connection
Shared information helps people learn about other cultures.
Television contributes to globalization or the “global village.”

Body Paragraph 3: Television and entertainment
Entertainment can teach moral lessons and show consequences.
But TV can also distort reality.

Conclusion
Restate why television has had a major impact.
Briefly mention both benefits and limits.

Improve sentence control

Many of your sentences are run-on sentences: several ideas are joined together with commas, dashes, or semicolons. This creates confusion.

For example, this sentence is too long:

> “While previously people had to wait days... now they can see it happening... this allows them...”

This should be divided into two or three sentences.

Revision strategy:
Express one main idea per sentence.
Use periods more often.
Use semicolons only when joining two complete, closely related sentences.
Avoid using & in formal academic writing; write and.

Make your thesis more precise

Your first sentence gives the topic, but the essay would be stronger with a more direct thesis.

For example:

Possible thesis:
> Television is one of the most important inventions of the twentieth century because it changed how people receive news, understand other cultures, and learn through entertainment.

This tells the reader exactly what the essay will argue.

Clarify and formalize some wording

Some expressions are too informal or unclear for academic writing.

Examples:
“every person in the world can watch this event”  
  This is too absolute. Not everyone has access.  
  Better: “people around the world can often watch major events live.”

“well, at least everybody who watches”  
  Too conversational.  
  Better: remove it or rewrite formally.

“thru”  
  Use through.

“making life seem the good guy always wins”  
  This needs correction.  
  Better: “making life seem as if the good guy always wins.”

“one journalist”  
  If you do not name the journalist, the phrase sounds vague. You could simply say:  
  “This idea is often described as a ‘global village.’”

Develop the counterargument more carefully

You mention that TV can glorify violence and present unrealistic versions of life. This is a good counterpoint. However, your response is not fully convincing yet.

You write:
> “However, this sense of reality is not so distorted...”

This idea is not clearly expressed. You could explain more directly:

> Although television sometimes presents unrealistic stories and excessive violence, most viewers can still distinguish fiction from reality. Therefore, television remains valuable when used critically.

This is clearer and stronger.

Work on grammar and punctuation details

Here are some common issues in the essay:

Spacing and punctuation: avoid extra spaces before punctuation.
Hyphenation:  
  - second- or third-hand accounts is correct.  
  - well-developed is correct.
Article use: sometimes you need the or a for smoother grammar.
Parallel structure: keep similar forms together in lists.

Example:
> “ideas, images, news, etc”
This is acceptable, but in formal writing, it is better to avoid etc if possible. Name the categories clearly.

Example of a revised opening

> One invention of the twentieth century that has significantly changed people’s lives is television. Television has transformed modern society by providing immediate access to news, connecting people across cultures, and offering entertainment that can also teach important lessons.

This version is more direct and academic.

Final advice

Your essay has good content and strong potential. The main revision goal is not to invent new ideas, but to present your ideas more clearly and formally. Focus especially on:
shorter sentences,
clearer paragraph organization,
a stronger thesis,
more formal vocabulary and punctuation.

After revising, read your essay aloud. If one sentence feels too long to say comfortably, it is probably too long and should be divided.

You have a solid argument—now make it easier for the reader to follow.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a relatable personal example. You show that you understand the assignment’s focus on how technology affects daily life, and your use of family experience makes the argument easy to follow. To improve the essay, focus on stronger organization, clearer argument, and sentence-level accuracy.

Main strengths

Clear topic
You clearly choose the microwave as an example of a technological advance that has affected people’s lives.

Relevant personal examples
Your discussion of your family’s cooking routine helps support your point that microwaves save time.

Awareness of another viewpoint
You also mention that not everyone thinks a microwave is necessary, which is good for an argumentative essay because it shows you recognize another perspective.

---

Main areas to improve

A. Make your thesis more direct
Right now, the essay begins generally, but your main argument is not stated strongly enough. A thesis should clearly tell the reader what you believe.

For example, you could write:

“One of the most useful technological inventions of the 20th century is the microwave because it saves time, makes meal preparation easier, and helps busy families manage their daily routines.”

This would give your essay a clearer direction.

---

B. Improve organization
Your essay has good ideas, but they are not arranged in the clearest argumentative structure. Try this pattern:

Introduction
   - General statement about technology
   - Specific focus on microwaves
   - Thesis

Body Paragraph 1
   - Explain how microwaves help busy families
   - Give your family example

Body Paragraph 2
   - Present the opposite opinion
   - Explain why some people do not want microwaves
   - Respond to that opinion

Conclusion
   - Restate your main point
   - End with a final comment about why microwaves remain valuable

At the moment, the essay ends with the story, but it needs a stronger conclusion that ties the argument together.

---

C. Develop the counterargument more carefully
You introduce an opposing view through your great uncle, which is effective. However, after giving his opinion, you should explain why you still disagree.

For example:
Some people believe microwaves encourage people to rush and reduce appreciation for cooking.
However, microwaves do not replace cooking completely; they simply make some tasks quicker and more practical for busy households.

This would make your essay more argumentative, instead of only descriptive.

---

D. Work on grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that you should revise. Here are some important ones:

Apostrophes and possession
“peoples lives” → “people’s lives”

Missing punctuation
“For instance a microwave…” → “For instance, a microwave…”

Sentence structure
“In today’s society it is common, in the ‘average’ family for both parents to work.”
  - Better: “In today’s society, it is common for both parents in an average family to work.”

Word form / spelling
“left overs” → “leftovers”
“recieved” → “received”
“micro-wave” → “microwave”

Verb form
“we each take turns cook” → “we each take turns cooking”  
  or “we take turns cooking”

Contractions / spacing
“they' re” → “they’re”

Redundant expression
“the point is, is that” → “the point is that”

Clarity
“an appliance such as a microwave is an unnecessary addition to their household”
  - Better: “a microwave is an unnecessary appliance in the home”

---

Style suggestions

Be a little more formal
Since this is an academic argumentative essay, try to avoid very conversational phrases such as:
“I have a little story to tell”
“the long and short of the story”
“pop them into the microwave”

These are natural in speech, but in formal writing you could replace them with:
“For example”
“This example shows that…”
“place them in the microwave”

Use topic sentences
Start each paragraph with a sentence that expresses the paragraph’s main idea. This will make your argument easier to follow.

For example:
“Microwaves are especially useful for families with busy schedules.”
“However, some people believe microwaves are unnecessary and encourage a rushed lifestyle.”

---

Content suggestions for revision
To strengthen the essay, you could add one or two more reasons why microwaves are useful:
they save energy compared with using a stove for small tasks
they are convenient for reheating food
they help students and workers prepare quick meals

Be careful not to rely only on one anecdote. Personal experience is good, but combining it with broader reasoning will make your essay stronger.

---

Final advice
You have a solid starting point: a clear example, a personal connection, and an opposing view. In revision, focus on:
writing a stronger thesis,
organizing the essay into clear paragraphs,
explaining the counterargument more fully,
correcting grammar and spelling errors,
using a more formal academic tone.

With these changes, your essay will become much clearer and more convincing.

These are strong ideas for an argumentative essay. To revise successfully, focus on organization, precision, grammar, and stronger argument development.

Strengths

Clear position: From the beginning, the reader understands that you believe ATMs are an important invention.
Relevant reasons: You give several useful benefits: emergency access to money, convenience, time-saving, and reduced need to carry cash.
Personal example: Your grocery store example helps make the argument concrete and believable.
Awareness of opposing view: You mention that some people worry ATMs reduce bank employment. This is good argumentative writing because it shows you recognize another perspective.

Main Areas to Improve

A. Make the thesis more direct
Your introduction presents the topic, but the main argument could be stated more clearly in one sentence.

Current idea:  
“An invention of the 20th century which I feel has significantly changed people's lives is the introduction of Bank-cash machines or Automatic teller machines.”

This is understandable, but it is long and slightly awkward.

Possible revision:  
“One of the most important inventions of the twentieth century is the automatic teller machine (ATM) because it gives people convenient, fast, and safer access to their money.”

This version is more direct and previews your main reasons.

B. Improve paragraph structure
Each body paragraph should focus on one main reason.

For example:
Paragraph 1: Convenience and emergency access
Paragraph 2: Time-saving and 24-hour availability
Paragraph 3: Safety and reduced need to carry cash
Counterargument paragraph: Some jobs may be affected, but the benefits outweigh the disadvantages

Right now, several ideas are mixed together in the same paragraph. Clear paragraphing will make your essay easier to follow.

C. Develop the argument more fully
Some points are good but need more explanation. For example:
Why is saving time especially important for working people?
How do ATMs help in emergencies?
Why is keeping less cash at home safer?

Try to explain each reason in 2–3 sentences, not just mention it.

D. Use formal academic language
Some expressions are too informal or repetitive.

Examples:
“I can not comprehend how difficult it would be...” → too conversational
“This is when I really appreciate my ATM card.” → acceptable, but a more formal explanation would be stronger

Try using more formal phrases such as:
“It is difficult to imagine daily life without this technology.”
“This feature is especially useful in urgent situations.”
“As a result, ATMs have become an essential part of modern life.”

Grammar and Style Corrections

Here are important sentence-level issues to revise:

Word choice / phrasing
“Bank-cash machines” → not standard. Use “automatic teller machines (ATMs)”
“a convenience at access to money” → better: “convenient access to money”
“working men and women of the late 1900's” → better: “working people in modern society”
“assigned banking hours” → better: “regular banking hours”
“appear in all areas of the World” → better: “are found throughout the world”

Grammar
“I can not” → “I cannot”
“ATM machines” is redundant because M = machine. Better: “ATMs”
“every day life” → “everyday life”
Be careful with capitalization:
  - “World” should usually be “world”
  - “Automatic teller machines” should not be capitalized in the middle of a sentence unless abbreviated as ATMs

Punctuation
There are a few run-on or awkwardly joined sentences. For example:

Original:  
“People may argue that ATM machines will take away employment in banks but we must also focus on the positive aspects.”

Improved:  
“Some people argue that ATMs reduce employment in banks, but we must also consider their positive effects.”

Also remove the unusual dash at the end:
“-- The development...” should either begin a new sentence or be integrated into the conclusion.

Suggestions for a Better Conclusion

Your conclusion repeats your opinion, which is good, but it can be stronger if it summarizes your main reasons more clearly.

Possible revised conclusion:  
“In conclusion, ATMs have greatly improved daily life by providing quick access to money, saving time, and increasing personal safety. Although some people worry about their effect on bank jobs, their advantages have made them one of the most useful inventions of modern technology.”

Next Revision Goals

When you rewrite, try to do these 5 things:
Write a clear thesis statement in the introduction.
Organize the essay into separate body paragraphs by reason.
Add more explanation for each point.
Use formal academic vocabulary.
Correct grammar, capitalization, and awkward phrases.

Overall
This essay has a solid foundation: a clear topic, a definite opinion, and relevant supporting ideas. The next step is to make the writing more organized, formal, and polished. With revision, this can become a much stronger argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and an engaging personal voice. You introduce the cellular telephone as an important discovery and discuss both its benefits and drawbacks. This gives your essay balance, which is a strength in argumentative writing. You also include a personal example, which makes the topic vivid and interesting. For revision, focus on making your main argument clearer, improving organization, and polishing grammar and style.

Main argument and focus
Your thesis is not fully consistent. In the introduction, you say cellular telephones are “a complete luxury and not a necessity,” but later you also describe them as “one of the most incredible 20th century discoveries” and “a mark of great expansion in technology.” These ideas are not impossible to combine, but you need to explain your position more clearly.

Ask yourself:  
Are you mainly arguing that cell phones are useful but nonessential?  
Or that they are an impressive invention despite some negative effects?  
Or that they are more harmful than beneficial?

Choose one central claim and make it explicit in the introduction. For example:

Although cellular telephones are an impressive technological invention, they are still more of a luxury than a necessity for most people.
Cellular telephones have greatly improved communication, but they also create dangers and unnecessary expense.

This will help the rest of the essay stay focused.

Organization
Your body paragraphs contain good ideas, but the structure can be improved. Right now, some topics are mixed together. For example, the third paragraph discusses both car accidents and cost. These are two different negative effects and would be clearer in separate paragraphs.

A stronger structure could be:

Introduction: introduce the topic and present your thesis  
Body paragraph 1: advantages of cellular telephones (constant communication, emergencies, travel)  
Body paragraph 2: disadvantages related to driving safety  
Body paragraph 3: disadvantages related to cost/luxury  
Body paragraph 4: personal example showing the power of the technology  
Conclusion: restate your opinion and end with a thoughtful final comment

Also, the last paragraph about robots feels somewhat disconnected from the argument. It is interesting, but it would work better if you linked it more directly to your conclusion about technological progress.

Development of ideas
You make several good points, but some need more explanation. For instance, when you say cell phones are a luxury, explain why. Is it because people can live without them? Because they are expensive? Because many people use them for convenience rather than necessity?

Similarly, your point about accidents would be stronger with a fuller explanation:
Why does phone use distract drivers?
How does this create danger for others?
Does this negative effect challenge the idea that cell phones improve life?

Try to connect each example back to your main claim.

Tone and style
Your voice is lively and personal, which is good, but some expressions are too informal for an academic essay. Examples include:
“&” → use and
“a bit ridiculous” → perhaps somewhat excessive
“lots of people” → many people
“just around the corner” is acceptable in informal writing, but for a more academic tone, you might say likely in the near future

Also avoid excessive punctuation such as “!!”. One exclamation mark is usually enough, and in formal essays it is often better to avoid them altogether.

Grammar and sentence issues
Here are some important corrections:

20th century discoveries → 20th-century discoveries  
the main way being the availability to be reached at any time  
  → the main one is that people can be reached at any time
if they are stranded or broken down  
  → if their car breaks down or if they are stranded
their friends houses  
  → their friends’ houses
people who are talking on their car phone  
  → people who are talking on their car phones
Although most people who can afford to buy a cellular phone, can afford to pay the bills!  
  → Remove the comma and exclamation mark:  
  Most people who can afford to buy a cellular phone can also afford to pay the bills.
it' s  
  → it’s

Be careful with punctuation. You sometimes use semicolons and dashes awkwardly. Simpler sentence structures may help you express your ideas more clearly.

Strongest parts
Clear topic
Balanced discussion of positives and negatives
Effective personal example
Engaging conclusion about future technology

Revision priorities
Write a clearer thesis that matches the whole essay.
Separate your ideas into more organized paragraphs.
Explain your reasons more fully.
Use a more formal academic tone.
Correct grammar, punctuation, and word choice.

Overall, this essay shows strong potential. You have interesting ideas and a confident voice. With a clearer argument and more careful organization, your essay will become much more persuasive.
